## 2026-08-26T13:00Z — run 1 (first live authoring run)

**Selection.** `tools/sync-state.mjs` first, as required: 6 structures covered by 2 scene files, STATE.json
already in step, cursor at `gross / Back & Vertebral Column / Vertebral column`. `DEMAND.json` does not
exist, so this run was **cursor-driven, not demand-driven**. Structures attempted: **Vertebral column** and
**Typical vertebra** (both `preferred_modes: ["3d_anatomy","diagram"]` → authored as `3d_anatomy`,
`deferred_modes: ["diagram"]`).

**Models resolved** (all looked up by catalog `name`, none copied from another scene):
- Cervical — atlas FMA12519, axis FMA12520, C3 FMA12521, C4 FMA12522, C5 FMA12523, C6 FMA12524, C7 FMA12525
- Thoracic — T1 FMA9165, T2 FMA9187, T3 FMA9209, T4 FMA9248, T5 FMA9922, T6 FMA9945, T7 FMA9968,
  T8 FMA9991, T9 FMA10014, T10 FMA10037, T11 FMA10059, T12 FMA10081
- Lumbar — L1 FMA13072, L2 FMA13073, L3 FMA13074, L4 FMA13075, L5 FMA13076; sacrum FMA16202
- Lumbar discs — FMA16033, FMA16034, FMA16035, FMA16036, FMA16037

**Models NOT found.**
- **Coccyx** — no entry under any spelling. The fifth region is narrated, never shown. Recorded in `gaps[]`.
- **Sub-parts of a vertebra** — body, pedicle, lamina, vertebral foramen, spinous / transverse / articular
  processes. The catalog's smallest unit is a whole vertebra; no sub-part exists for any of the 24. This is
  the reason the Typical vertebra scene is not `ready`.

**Judgement call — Typical vertebra held at `candidate`.** The honest move for the missing sub-parts is
landmark anchors on the L2 mesh, as the arm scene does for its six bony landmarks. Those anchors are
*measured* on the source geometry (`tools/derive-landmarks.html`), and the vertebral meshes are not in
`viz-training/meshes/` while this task has no network access. Placing anchors by estimate would put a label
on the wrong process — the exact failure the canonical-name check exists to prevent — so none were placed.
`covers: ["Typical vertebra"]` is declared but `status:"candidate"` keeps the structure on the worklist,
which is why the cursor still reads `Typical vertebra` after the run. Fetch the meshes, derive the anchors,
add them as `role:"part"` / `render:"anchor"`, and the scene becomes ready with no other edit.

**Validator** — `validate-scenes.mjs --mark`: **4/4 valid, 0 errors, 0 warnings**, all 8 stages clean.
- `gross__back-vertebral-column__vertebral-column.json` · 30 structures (25 parts) · 6 views · 21 ops ·
  ready · degrades: PEEL_LAYER
- `gross__back-vertebral-column__typical-vertebra.json` · 7 structures (3 parts) · 5 views · 18 ops · candidate
- Nothing blocked. No scene rewritten by `--mark`.

**Index** — `build-scene-index.mjs`: index.json written · 4 scenes (3 ready) · 186 term mappings (was 103).

**State** — `sync-state.mjs` re-run: 8 structures covered by 4 scene files; gross 7/81; STATE.json
reconciled. Next to author: `gross / Back & Vertebral Column / Typical vertebra` (the candidate above).

## 2026-08-26T13:09Z — run 2

**Selection.** `tools/sync-state.mjs` first, as required: 8 structures covered by 4 scene files, STATE.json
already in step, cursor at `gross / Back & Vertebral Column / Typical vertebra`. `DEMAND.json` does not
exist, so this run was **cursor-driven, not demand-driven**.

The cursor pointed at **Typical vertebra**, which already has a scene on disk held at `candidate` from run 1
— correct behaviour, since only a `ready` scene is credited as covered. It was **not re-authored**. Its one
blocker is unchanged: the sub-parts of a vertebra need measured landmark anchors, the measurement needs the
source geometry, and `viz-training/meshes/` still holds only the 33 arm and heart meshes — no vertebral STL
is present and this task has no network access. Re-authoring it from the same inputs would have produced the
same scene. It stays on the worklist and the run moved to the next two unauthored structures:
**Intervertebral disc** and **Spinal cord in vertebral canal** (both `preferred_modes:
["3d_anatomy","diagram"]` → authored as `3d_anatomy`, `deferred_modes: ["diagram"]`).

**Models resolved** (each looked up by the catalog's own `name`; the ids in the second scene were emitted
directly from `available-meshes.json` by id→name lookup rather than typed, so stage 2 cannot drift):
- Discs — L4/5 FMA16036, L5/S1 FMA16037, T6/7 FMA13503, C5/6 FMA13898, L1/2 FMA16033, L2/3 FMA16034,
  L3/4 FMA16035
- Vertebrae — C1 FMA12519, C2 FMA12520, C3 FMA12521, C4 FMA12522, C5 FMA12523, C6 FMA12524, C7 FMA12525;
  T1 FMA9165, T2 FMA9187, T3 FMA9209, T4 FMA9248, T5 FMA9922, T6 FMA9945, T7 FMA9968, T8 FMA9991,
  T9 FMA10014, T10 FMA10037, T11 FMA10059, T12 FMA10081; L1 FMA13072, L2 FMA13073, L3 FMA13074,
  L4 FMA13075, L5 FMA13076; sacrum FMA16202
- Cord — `FMA78497` **central canal of spinal cord** (the only entry in the region; see below)

**Models NOT found.**
- **The spinal cord itself.** Searched the 934-mesh catalog for cord, dura, arachnoid, pia, meninges,
  cauda, filum, conus, nerve, ganglion, "vertebral canal". The only hits are `FMA78497` central canal of
  spinal cord, `FMA62004` medulla oblongata, and the two optic nerves. There is no cord, no meninx, no
  spinal nerve and no root anywhere in the catalog.
- **Nucleus pulposus / annulus fibrosus** — no entry under either spelling. The whole disc is the smallest
  unit held.
- **Anterior and posterior longitudinal ligaments** — the catalog holds four ligaments in total, all in the
  limbs (inguinal, long plantar).
- **Coccyx** — still absent, as in run 1.

**Judgement calls.**
1. *Intervertebral disc → `ready`.* Every mesh it names resolves and every name matches the catalog
   character for character. The missing nucleus/annulus and the missing nerve root are narrated and
   recorded in `gaps[]`; they limit how literal two beats are but not whether the structure is taught, so
   the scene is not held back. `covers: ["Intervertebral disc"]` only.
2. *Spinal cord in vertebral canal → `candidate`.* Option (b) of the resolve rule — teach through an
   adjacent structure that exists. The bony canal is complete and carries most of the exam content, and the
   central canal is included as an honestly labelled proxy for the cord's axis, never as "the cord". Two
   guards were put on that decision: the extent of the central-canal mesh is unverified offline, so **no
   teaching depends on it** (cord termination is taught from L1/L2, which are measured bone), and the scene
   stays `candidate`, which keeps it off the student route and on the worklist. `covers` is declared as
   intent; `sync-state.mjs` credits only `ready` scenes, so nothing is hidden by it.
3. *TRACE_STRUCTURE subjects* use the declared abstraction: `concept:disc-load` and `concept:spinal-cord`.
   The second is the honest form of "trace something with no mesh" — the day a cord model exists it becomes
   a real target with no other edit.

**Validator** — `validate-scenes.mjs --mark`: **6/6 valid, 0 errors, 0 warnings**, all 8 stages clean.

- `gross__back-vertebral-column__spinal-cord-in-vertebral-canal.json` · 28 structures (27 parts) · 7 views ·
  26 ops · candidate
- Nothing blocked. No scene rewritten by `--mark`.

**Index** — `build-scene-index.mjs`: index.json written · 6 scenes (4 ready) · 280 term mappings (was 186).

**State** — `sync-state.mjs` re-run: 10 structures covered by 6 scene files; gross 8/81; STATE.json
reconciled. Next to author still reads `gross / Back & Vertebral Column / Typical vertebra` — the run-1
candidate. It will keep reading that until the vertebral meshes are fetched and its anchors derived; a
future run should either do that or, if still offline, skip past it to the next unauthored structure as this
run did.

**Note for whoever fetches meshes next.** Three scenes are now waiting on the same thing — vertebral STLs
for the Typical vertebra anchors, and any cord model at all. `tools/fetch-scene-meshes.mjs` exists; this
task is not permitted to run it (no network), so it needs a human or a networked job.

## 2026-08-26T14:08Z — run 3 · Erector spinae / deep back muscles · Pectoralis major

**Demand.** `DEMAND.json` does not exist, so this run was **cursor-driven**, not demand-driven.

**Cursor.** `sync-state.mjs` ran first and reported 10 structures covered by 6 scene files, next to author
`gross / Back & Vertebral Column / Spinal cord in vertebral canal`. That structure **already has a scene on
disk** (`candidate`, authored run 2) and the cursor keeps pointing at it because `sync-state.mjs` credits
only `ready` scenes. Re-authoring it would have produced the same file for the same reason — the catalog has
no spinal cord — so this run **skipped past it to the next two unauthored structures**, exactly as run 2's
note recommended. It stays on the worklist and is listed under *still waiting* below.

**Structures attempted.**
1. `gross / Back & Vertebral Column / Erector spinae / deep back muscles` — `preferred_modes`
   `["3d_anatomy","diagram"]` → **3d_anatomy**, `deferred_modes: ["diagram"]`.
2. `gross / Pectoral Region & Breast / Pectoralis major` — `preferred_modes` `["3d_anatomy","diagram"]` →
   **3d_anatomy**, `deferred_modes: ["diagram"]`.

**Models resolved — erector spinae (33 structures, 19 parts).** All looked up by catalog `name`.
- Iliocostalis: FMA22740 lumborum · FMA22742 thoracis · FMA22744 cervicis
- Longissimus: FMA22751 thoracis · FMA22757 cervicis · FMA22754 capitis
- Spinalis: FMA22779 thoracis · FMA22781 cervicis
- Transversospinalis: FMA22876/22874/22872 semispinalis capitis/cervicis/thoracis · FMA22878 multifidus ·
  FMA23083 thoracic rotator · FMA23089 lumbar rotator · FMA81752 cervical rotator ·
  FMA74075/74077 levatores costarum longi/breves
- Splenius: FMA22728 capitis · FMA22726 cervicis
- Context: FMA33586/33584/33581 trapezius parts · FMA13358 latissimus dorsi · FMA13381/13383 rhomboids ·
  FMA13403/13405 serratus posterior superior/inferior · FMA16202 sacrum · FMA16586 hip bone ·
  FMA8175 sixth rib · FMA8533 twelfth rib · FMA52735 occipital bone · FMA52738 temporal bone

**Models resolved — pectoralis major (19 structures, 3 parts).**
- Heads: FMA34690 clavicular part · FMA79979 sternocostal part · FMA45874 abdominal part
- Origins: FMA13322 clavicle · FMA7486 manubrium · FMA7487 body of sternum ·
  FMA7886/7913/7976/8070/8194 costal cartilages 2–6 · FMA13336 external oblique · FMA13377 rectus abdominis
- Insertion: FMA23130 humerus
- Deep/neighbouring: FMA13375 pectoralis minor · FMA13412 subclavius · FMA13398 serratus anterior ·
  FMA34680 clavicular part of deltoid · FMA13358 latissimus dorsi

**Models NOT found.**
- **"Erector spinae" as one mesh** — absent. Searched `/erector|spinae/` → 0 hits for the whole muscle; the
  catalog's smallest units are the nine named column parts, all of which exist. Resolve rule option (a).
- **Spinalis capitis** — absent (it is inconstant in real bodies and blends with semispinalis capitis, which
  is present). Iliocostalis correctly has no capitis part.
- **All fascia** — `/thoracolumbar|fascia|aponeurosis/` returns only the epicranial aponeurosis and the two
  tensor fasciae latae. No thoracolumbar fascia, no erector spinae aponeurosis, no ligamentum nuchae, no
  pectoral or clavipectoral fascia.
- **All nerves** — still only the two optic nerves. No dorsal rami (the definition of an intrinsic back
  muscle), no lateral or medial pectoral nerve.
- **Axillary vessels** — `/axillary/` → 0 hits, `/cephalic/` matches only the brachiocephalic veins, and
  there is no thoracoacromial artery. The cephalic vein in the deltopectoral groove is narrated.
- **Breast** — `/mammary|breast|nipple/` → **0 hits.** This blocks curriculum entry
  "Breast (mammary gland)" (1.2) as a 3D scene entirely; it should take its deferred `diagram` mode.
- **Coracoid process, bicipital groove** — no separate meshes; narrated on the scapula and humerus.

**Judgement calls.**
1. *Skipping the cursor structure.* The task's own opening warning is against authoring a second copy of
   something already on disk. `Spinal cord in vertebral canal` is on disk and `candidate` for a reason that
   no amount of re-authoring changes. Skipped, recorded, left on the worklist.
2. *Erector spinae → `ready`.* Assembled from its nine real parts rather than held back for a whole-muscle
   mesh that does not exist. Every name matches the catalog character for character. `covers` is the single
   curriculum entry it is authored for.
3. *No PEEL_LAYER in the erector spinae scene*, despite the instruction to author ops the teaching needs.
   The spec's layer vocabulary (skin/fascia/muscle/vessel/organ/bone) cannot express the three dissection
   layers this structure is actually taught in, and every muscle here is `layer: "muscle"`, so a peel would
   clear the whole scene at once rather than reveal the next layer. The layer dissection is authored with
   HIDE_STRUCTURE on groups — native, and it does exactly what the teaching needs. Recorded in `gaps[]` with
   the note that beats 2 and 3 become PEEL_LAYER unchanged if the vocabulary ever gains dissection depth.
4. *`thoracic rotator` (FMA23083) is unsided* while the cervical and lumbar rotators are sided. Rather than
   drop the best-developed rotatores or let a bilateral mesh masquerade as a right-sided muscle, it is
   included and **labelled** "Rotatores (thoracic) — both sides in one model". Interspinales and
   intertransversarii, which exist only as unsided sets, are omitted and named in narration instead.
5. *Pectoralis major `covers` only itself.* Pectoralis minor renders in the scene as context but its own
   curriculum entry teaches the axilla and the three parts of the axillary artery, which this scene does not
   do. Marking it covered would have hidden a real gap.

**Ledger correction.** CORPUS.md listed `Typical vertebra` as 🟡 candidate. The file on disk is
`status: "ready"` with 16 landmark anchors and the validator agrees, so the row was stale — a second
bookkeeping copy drifting from the scenes directory, which is the thing this pipeline is built not to
tolerate. Row corrected to ✅ ready / 16 landmarks. The run-1 note below it is left as written, since it is
an accurate record of what was true that run.

**Still waiting on a networked job.** Unchanged and now larger: any spinal cord model at all (1 scene held
at `candidate`), and — new this run — there is no point fetching a breast mesh because the catalog has no
entry to fetch. Curriculum entry 1.2 "Breast (mammary gland)" needs either a different provider or its

## Run 2026-08-26T15:0Z (run 4) — cursor-driven

**Demand** — `viz-training/DEMAND.json` does not exist, so this run was **not** demand-driven. Structures
were taken from the cursor after `sync-state.mjs`, which reported `next to author: gross / Pectoral Region
& Breast / Pectoralis minor` and confirmed STATE.json was already in step with the 8 scenes on disk.

### 2 · Breast (mammary gland) → `gross__pectoral-region-breast__breast-mammary-gland` · diagram · **planned**
`preferred_modes: ["3d_anatomy","diagram"]`. No engine can render 3d_anatomy for this structure today
because **nothing in the catalog matches it**: `breast` 0, `mammary` 0, `nipple` 0, `areola` 0, `lactif` 0.
So the second mode was taken — `mode: "diagram"`, `provider.primary: "svg"`, no `refs` on any structure —
and **`3d_anatomy` was kept in `deferred_modes` rather than being dropped from the record**. This is the
run-3 prediction coming true and being honoured rather than worked around: curriculum intent outlives
provider coverage.

Status is **`planned`, not `ready`**. The scene is fully authored — 14 structures, 6 beats, 19 ops, full
narration — but no SVG artwork exists for it, so it renders nothing to a student. Promoting it would be the
same lie as marking a structure covered that a scene cannot teach.

**Decisions made autonomously** (no user present):
1. *Right side throughout the pectoralis minor scene*, matching the pectoralis major scene authored in run 3,
   so a student moving between the two scenes is not mirrored.
2. *`concept:arterial_blood` and `concept:milk`* used as TRACE targets where the subject is a flow rather
   than a model — the declared abstraction in the spec, not a bare word.
3. *PEEL_LAYER authored in beat 1* of the pectoralis minor scene. It degrades on the current adapter, and it
   is the right op: here the layers genuinely differ (`muscle` over `muscle` is the wrong critique — the
   teaching move is stripping pectoralis major off, and the degraded behaviour of hiding the muscle layer
   still shows the minor beneath the bones). Recorded as a degrade, not a gap.
4. *The breast scene declares `camera.framing: "sagittal"`*, which is outside the six rotation views of the
   op vocabulary. It is inert for the SVG engine and is recorded in `gaps[]` as a note to a future 3D
   version about which plane the teaching assumes.

(was 8 scenes / 556).

asymmetry, which is correct: 10 files, 14 covered, but only 12 of the 14 count toward gross's readiness

`FMA4751 right brachiocephalic vein` · `FMA7857 right first rib` · `FMA32551 right teres major` ·

**Models NOT found — this is the whole problem with this entry:**

- `lymph` — **0 hits in the entire catalog.** Not one node, anywhere in the body.

  subscapular, anterior and posterior circumflex humeral — 0 hits.

  subclavian lymph trunk, axillary sheath, clavipectoral fascia, internal thoracic / parasternal nodes,
  long thoracic nerve, brachial plexus cords, breast — 0 hits each.

**Why `candidate` and not `ready`.** The validator passes it, but passing the gate is necessary, not
sufficient: the authoring rules say a scene that could not be fully resolved is `candidate`. Every one of
the three subjects this curriculum entry names is narrated rather than shown. `covers` still declares
`["Axillary vessels & lymph nodes"]`, which is correct — `sync-state.mjs` treats a non-ready scene as HELD,
so the structure stays on the worklist and stays visible instead of quietly disappearing into coverage.

**Decisions made autonomously** (no user present):
1. *Right side throughout*, matching runs 3 and 4, so a student moving between pectoral scenes is not mirrored.
2. *Option (b) over option (c) for the axillary vessels*: the subclavian artery and vein are shown and the
   name changes narrated at the first rib and at teres major, rather than omitting the vessels entirely.
   They are literally the same tubes; teaching the borders is the examinable content.
3. *No guessed anchors, in either scene.* This is the significant call of the run. Anchors were the obvious
   fix for both the clavicle's landmarks and the five nodal groups, and the spec sanctions authored markers
   for parts with no model — but every existing anchor carries a measured `calibrated_by`, and neither mesh
   set is available locally to measure. An unmeasured anchor marked `needs-review` would still have put
   invented coordinates into the corpus. Narration plus an explicit `gaps[]` entry was chosen instead.
4. *`concept:arterial_blood` and `concept:lymph`* as TRACE targets where the subject is a flow, not a model.
5. *A non-schema `candidate_reason` field* on the axillary scene, stating in one paragraph why it is held.
   The validator ignores unknown fields; the reason belongs next to the status rather than only in a log.
6. *PEEL_LAYER authored in the clavicle scene's beat 4* — it degrades today and is still the right op for
   stripping the muscles off the bone.

## Run 2026-08-26T17:07Z (run 6) — `Brachial plexus` · `Axillary artery`

**Driver: cursor, not demand.** `viz-training/DEMAND.json` does not exist, so the run fell back to the
cursor as instructed. `sync-state.mjs` ran first, before anything was read or decided, and reported
STATE.json already in step: 16 covered by 12 files, `next to author: gross / Axilla & Brachial Plexus /
Brachial plexus`. Nothing was taken from memory.

**The decision this run turned on.** Both structures carry `preferred_modes: ["3d_anatomy", "diagram"]`.
The catalog was searched for every subject either entry names, and it holds none of them:

- `plexus|nerve|root|trunk|cord` over all 934 entries returns **two optic nerves and two choroid plexuses**.
  There is no nerve of the upper limb in the catalog at all — no root, no trunk, no cord, no named branch.
- `axillary|thoracoacromial|circumflex humeral|subscapular artery` returns **nothing**. The nearest models
  are `right subclavian artery` (FMA3953) and `right subclavian vein` (FMA4755), which are the proximal
  continuations, not the vessel itself.

So the first mode an engine can render today is `diagram` for both, provider `svg`, `structures[]` as
labelled regions with no refs, `status: "planned"` — exactly the route the task prescribes and the same one
the breast scene took in run 4. `3d_anatomy` was **not** removed from either curriculum entry; it is
recorded in `deferred_modes` on both scenes and the reason is written into `gaps[]`. Provider coverage did
not get to edit the curriculum.

**Why the axillary artery is a diagram and not a second 3D axilla scene.** Option (b) — teach it through
adjacent structures that exist — was available: subclavian artery, first rib, teres major, pectoralis
minor, the four walls. But that scene already exists. Run 5 authored
`gross__pectoral-region-breast__axillary-vessels-lymph-nodes` from precisely those models, and its own
`gaps[]` records that the 1-2-3 branch rule "is the most examinable fact in this entry and it currently has
no picture." A second 3D scene from the same handful of wall muscles would have duplicated run 5 without
teaching anything new. The diagram is the missing picture: the 3D scene teaches the space, this one teaches
the branching tree that has no models. That reasoning is written into the scene's `gaps[]` so the next
reader does not undo it.

### 1 · Brachial plexus → `gross__axilla-brachial-plexus__brachial-plexus` · diagram · **planned**

36 structures (32 parts) · 6 views · 30 ops · `covers: ["Brachial plexus"]`.

Roots C5–T1, three trunks, anterior and posterior divisions, three cords, four supraclavicular branches,
seven cord branches, five terminal branches, and three lesions authored as annotation regions beside the
level that produces them. Beats: where it runs (scalenes → first rib → behind the clavicle → axilla);
five-three-six-three-five traced with `concept:nerve_fibres` along the five stage groups; how the divisions
build the cords, named around the second part of the axillary artery; the M in the axilla; the branches
that leave early, with ULTRA for the posterior cord; and a last beat that reads the lesion backwards from
the deficit — Erb's, Klumpke's, winged scapula.

**Models resolved:** none, and none attempted — a diagram scene carries no refs and the validator skips
provider-id and existence for it.
**Models NOT found:** every nerve structure in the scene. Thirty of them.

### 2 · Axillary artery → `gross__axilla-brachial-plexus__axillary-artery` · diagram · **planned**

18 structures (13 parts) · 6 views · 31 ops · `covers: ["Axillary artery"]`.

Three parts divided by pectoralis minor, six branches counted one-two-three, and the relations that make
the artery the reference point of the axilla. Beats: the two landmarks where the name changes, traced with
`concept:arterial_blood` from subclavian to brachial; pectoralis minor cutting it in three; the 1-2-3 rule
tied branch by branch with SHOW_RELATIONSHIP; what travels with it, with `PEEL_LAYER` taking off the
axillary sheath to show artery and cords inside and the vein outside; where it may be tied, with
`concept:collateral_flow` walking the scapular anastomosis; and two branches that keep coming up clinically
— posterior circumflex humeral with the axillary nerve in the quadrangular space, lateral thoracic to the
breast.

**Models resolved:** none — diagram scene.
**Models NOT found:** axillary artery, superior thoracic, thoracoacromial, lateral thoracic, subscapular,
anterior and posterior circumflex humeral, axillary vein, axillary sheath, scapular anastomosis vessels.

**Validator** — `validate-scenes.mjs --mark`: **14/14 valid**, 0 blocked, 0 errors on either new scene.
All eight stages passed. Warnings only, and only `[capability]`: 30 + 31 lines of "op is unknown to the svg
adapter — it will be ignored, not degraded", because `CAPABILITIES.svg` still declares `native: []` and
`degraded: []`. This is now the third run to log it and the corpus has three diagram scenes carrying 80
ops between them. **It should be fixed before more diagram scenes are authored**, otherwise the capability
stage tells us nothing about what the SVG engine will actually do with an authored beat.

**Index** — `build-scene-index.mjs`: index.json written · **14 scenes (9 ready) · 1038 term mappings**
(was 12 / 9 / 838). Nine ready is unchanged — this run added no renderable scene, only two authored ones.

**State** — `sync-state.mjs` re-run: **18 structures covered by 14 scene files; gross 13/81.**
`next to author: gross / Axilla & Brachial Plexus / Axillary vein`.

**Cursor-driven, not demand-driven.** `viz-training/DEMAND.json` does not exist, so there is no student

in step — no drift this run.

### 1 · Axillary vein → `gross__axilla-brachial-plexus__axillary-vein` · diagram · **planned**

18 structures (17 parts) · 7 views · 33 ops · `covers: ["Axillary vein"]`.

Beats: born at one border and renamed at the other, traced with `concept:venous_blood` from basilic to
brachiocephalic; medial to the artery all the way, with the medial cord caught between the two; the
tributaries copying the artery's branches, plus the thoracoepigastric route to the femoral vein and Mondor
disease; the cephalic vein piercing the clavipectoral fascia, with `PEEL_LAYER`; a `CROSS_SECTION` reading
front to back that turns the one fact — the sheath wraps artery and cords but leaves the vein outside — into
two exam answers; the three things that go wrong (air embolism, effort thrombosis, lymphoedema); and the
infraclavicular approach to a central line.

**Models resolved:** none — diagram scene.
**Models NOT found:** axillary vein, basilic vein, cephalic vein, brachial venae comitantes, thoracoacromial
vein, lateral thoracic vein, thoracoepigastric vein, subscapular vein, circumflex humeral veins, axillary
sheath, clavipectoral fascia, any lymph node. A search of the catalog for "vein" returns 21 entries and not
one is in the upper limb below the subclavian.

**Duplication check.** This is the same question run 6 faced for the artery, and it was answered the same
way. `gross__pectoral-region-breast__axillary-vessels-lymph-nodes` (candidate) already shows this vein in
3D as `right subclavian vein` FMA4755, and its own `gaps[]` says the vein is "spoken rather than seen". A
third 3D scene built from the same fourteen wall-and-boundary meshes would have duplicated it. Recorded in
`gaps[]` on the new scene so the reasoning survives the run.

**One authoring choice worth flagging.** The vein is drawn as three segments named by their relation to
pectoralis minor, so the ops can address it at a given level. That is a teaching device borrowed from the
artery, **not** standard anatomy — the axillary vein is not conventionally divided into parts. The
narration never claims it is, and `gaps[]` says so explicitly.

### 2 · Axillary lymph nodes → `gross__axilla-brachial-plexus__axillary-lymph-nodes` · diagram · **planned**

18 structures (18 parts) · 7 views · 33 ops · `covers: ["Axillary lymph nodes"]`.

Beats: five groups, each on a wall of the pyramid; what each group drains, in three sentences; the order of
flow traced with `concept:lymph`; the surgeon's three levels laid across the same nodes by pectoralis
minor, with the warning that artery numbering runs downward and node numbering upward; breast spread traced
with `concept:tumour_spread` including the medial quarter to the internal thoracic nodes and the anatomy
behind sentinel node biopsy; clearance with `PEEL_LAYER` and the three nerves at risk; and palpating an
axilla properly.

**Models resolved:** none — diagram scene.
**Models NOT found:** **every single subject of the entry.** There is not one lymph node in the catalog, in
any region of the body — 934 entries searched for "lymph" and for "node", zero hits. Also missing:
lymphatic vessels, subclavian lymph trunk, breast, long thoracic nerve, thoracodorsal nerve,
intercostobrachial nerve. This is the first structure in the corpus where 3D was not a weaker option but no
option at all.

**This is the fourth consecutive run to log that warning and it should now be treated as a defect, not a
note.** `CAPABILITIES.svg` in `validate-scenes.mjs` still declares `native: []` and `degraded: []`. The

without emitting anything if any of those fail. Eleven landmarks, each printed with the measurement that

were first reported against the shaft axis, which made the lateral one look 44.5 mm prominent against the
medial one's 19.6 mm — an artefact of the distal end being offset from the shaft, and against the centre of
the distal end they come out 31.0 and 33.0 mm, which is the symmetry a real bone has. And the surgical neck
was first defined as the narrowest level in the proximal half, which is wrong in a way that would have
shipped: the humerus is narrowest at mid-shaft, so that definition walks the label down the arm. It is now
tied to the tubercles — the narrowest level in the 10% of bone directly below the lowest one.

limb. The only names containing "brachial" are muscles — `left/right brachialis`, `left/right

scene's `gaps[]` claimed brachialis and coracobrachialis were absent; that was wrong and was corrected
before the scene was saved.** They are present (FMA37668, FMA37665), and so is every other muscle and bone
the artery relates to. The honest statement, which is now what the scene says, is stronger: the entire bed
resolves and only the subject does not. The catalog's most distal
upper-limb arteries are `right subclavian artery` FMA3953 and `left subclavian artery` FMA4694, which are
two name-changes proximal to this vessel and cannot stand in for it without labelling one vessel as
another. That is the failure mode the whole v2 gate exists to prevent, so option (b) was refused here and
option (c) taken: routed to the deferred `diagram` mode with the absence written into `gaps[]`.

**Models NOT found — median, ulnar & radial nerves.** A search for `nerve` across all 934 entries returns
exactly two: `right optic nerve` FMA50875 and `left optic nerve` FMA50878. There is no median, ulnar,
radial, musculocutaneous or axillary nerve, and no nerve of any limb. Thirteen nerve structures in the
scene, none resolvable. Identical to the run-6 finding for the brachial plexus.

**A 3D version of the nerve scene was considered and rejected.** The humerus exists as a model and run 8
left eleven measured landmark anchors on it, including the radial groove and the medial epicondyle — so a
3D scene showing bone with nerve courses drawn between anchors was technically available. It was rejected
for two reasons. First, the run-8 humerus scene **already** traces `concept:radial_nerve` along neck →
groove → lateral epicondyle and pairs three fracture sites with three nerves; a second 3D scene over the
same bone would have duplicated that beat and taught nothing new. Second, this curriculum entry's stated
content is *motor/sensory territories*, and territories are not bone — a 3D scene would have shown the one
third that has geometry and narrated the two thirds that do not. The `gaps[]` array says all of this, and
records that when nerve models arrive, run 8's anchors are the waypoints the traces should be re-authored
against. `3d_anatomy` stays first in `preferred_modes` on both structures and is recorded in
`deferred_modes` on both scenes. Nothing in `CURRICULUM.json` was edited.

metacarpal.

**New tool — `tools/derive-forearm-landmarks.mjs`.** The catalog's smallest unit is a whole radius and a
whole ulna; there is no mesh for an olecranon or a styloid process. Rather than type anchors in by eye, the
run wrote a measuring tool in the same shape as `derive-humerus-landmarks.mjs`: it re-proves the LPS frame
on the meshes it is handed (both bones entirely at X<0, longest axis Z, ulna medial to radius proximally,
olecranon above the top of the radius) and refuses to emit anything if a check fails. Eleven landmarks, each
CONTACT (nearest point to a named neighbouring mesh) or EXTREME (furthest in a stated direction within a
stated slice), each carrying its measurement into `calibrated_by`:

- radius — head 4.85 mm from the humerus · radial tuberosity **0.10 mm** from the biceps tendon, 20.5 mm
  off the shaft axis · ulnar notch 1.45 mm from the ulna · radial styloid, most distal point, 41.7 mm off
  the shaft axis.
- ulna — olecranon, most proximal point, 17.5 mm above the top of the radius · trochlear notch 0.62 mm from
  the humerus · coronoid process, most anterior point of the proximal quarter · radial notch 1.67 mm from
  the radius · head of ulna 1.45 mm from the radius · ulnar styloid, most distal point.

All eleven are `status:"needs-review"` per the spec, awaiting a human clear in-app.

**One measured fact that contradicts the textbook, recorded rather than smoothed over.** On these meshes the
ulna's lowest point is **0.8 mm distal to the radial styloid** — the radial styloid does not sit ~1 cm lower,
as it does in life and as the Colles fracture test depends on. Identical in `meshes/` and `meshes-lite/`, so
it is the source segmentation and not the decimation. The scene teaches the relationship in narration
because it is examinable, and `gaps[]` says in as many words that a student must not read the offset off the
model.

**Models NOT found — recorded in `gaps[]`, not worked around silently.**
- **No median nerve mesh**, and none for any peripheral nerve or forearm artery. For the carpal tunnel this
  is the content of the tunnel, so beat 7 authors it as `TRACE_STRUCTURE` on `concept:median_nerve` along a
  path of real structures — the declared abstraction the spec provides, not a bare invented target.
- **No annular ligament, triangular fibrocartilage, oblique cord or elbow capsule** — the ligaments that
  hold both radioulnar pivots together are narration only.
- **No carpal meshes in the local mesh set**, and this run may not fetch. So the tubercle of the scaphoid,
  the tubercle of the trapezium and the hook of the hamate are taught by lighting the whole bone rather
  than by anchors. Deriving those four anchors is the obvious next improvement, and needs only the five
  carpal STLs locally.
- **No tendon meshes separate from muscle bellies**, no synovial sheaths, no palmar aponeurosis.

worth saying in the scene rather than pretending each muscle is separate. The palmar arches are vessels and
will be a gap. After that the cursor leaves the upper limb for `Gluteal Region & Hip Joint`.

## Run 2026-08-27 — Sciatic nerve · Gluteal vessels

**Timestamp** 2026-08-27 (scheduled run). Model-free, log-only. No app code touched, no commit, no push, no
network, no browser.

**Cursor or demand** — Cursor. `viz-training/DEMAND.json` does not exist on disk, so this run was **not**
demand-driven and fell back to the cursor as the prompt directs. `sync-state.mjs` ran first, before anything
was read or decided, and reported STATE.json already in step: 32 structures covered by 28 files, gross
23/81, `next to author: gross / Gluteal Region & Hip Joint / Sciatic nerve`.

**Structures attempted** — `Sciatic nerve` and `Gluteal vessels`, the last two entries in
`Gluteal Region & Hip Joint`. Both authored. **The topic is now complete: all five structures have scenes.**

**A decision that departs from the previous run's prediction.** Run 12 closed by predicting these two would
go to the diagram backlog, on the precedent of the axilla and arm scenes: no model, so drop to
`preferred_modes[1]`. That prediction was not followed, and the reason is worth recording because it is a
rule, not a one-off. The axillary artery and the three nerves of the arm had **nothing** to hang on — the
structures they run between are largely unmodelled too, so a 3D scene would have been a bare bone with a
line drawn in the air. The gluteal region is the opposite case: everything the sciatic nerve lies on is
modelled, in order, and the vessels' parent trunk is modelled. In this region the nerve and the arteries
are *defined by their relations* — that is exactly how they are taught and examined, because nothing here
can be palpated. So `3d_anatomy` earns its place: the trace runs across real geometry at real depth.
`diagram` is recorded in `deferred_modes` on both, and both `gaps[]` say plainly that a drawn diagram would
show the branching and the anastomoses better. The test applied was: *does dropping to a diagram lose more
than it gains?* Here it does. In the axilla it did not.

**Models resolved — sciatic nerve (25 structures, all names copied verbatim from the catalog):**
`right piriformis` FMA22340 · `right gemellus superior` FMA22334 · `right obturator internus` FMA22324 ·
`right gemellus inferior` FMA22336 · `right quadratus femoris` FMA22338 · `right gluteus maximus` FMA22328 ·
`right gluteus medius` FMA22330 · `right hip bone` FMA16586 · `sacrum` FMA16202 · `right femur` FMA24474 ·
`right fibula` FMA24480 · `long head of right biceps femoris` FMA45888 · `right semitendinosus` FMA22358 ·
`right semimembranosus` FMA22448 · `right adductor magnus` FMA22459 · `medial head of right gastrocnemius`
FMA45957 · `right soleus` FMA22558 · `right tibialis posterior` FMA65018 · `right flexor digitorum longus`
FMA65016 · `short head of right biceps femoris` FMA45891 · `right tibialis anterior` FMA22544 ·
`right extensor digitorum longus` FMA22548 · `right extensor hallucis longus` FMA22546 ·
`right fibularis longus` FMA22552 · `right fibularis brevis` FMA22554.

**Models resolved — gluteal vessels (17 structures):** `right internal iliac artery` FMA18809 ·
`right common iliac artery` FMA14765 · `right external iliac artery` FMA18806 · `descending aorta` FMA3784 ·
`right internal iliac vein` FMA18887 · `right common iliac vein` FMA21387 · `right piriformis` FMA22340 ·
`right gluteus medius` FMA22330 · `right gluteus minimus` FMA22332 · `right tensor fasciae latae` FMA22425 ·
`right gluteus maximus` FMA22328 · `right gemellus superior` FMA22334 · `right obturator internus` FMA22324 ·
`right quadratus femoris` FMA22338 · `right hip bone` FMA16586 · `sacrum` FMA16202 · `right femur` FMA24474.

**Models NOT found — searched and confirmed absent from all 934 catalog entries:**

- **No sciatic nerve, and no nerve of the lower limb at all.** Confirmed again by a full scan: the only
  nerve models in the catalog are `left optic nerve` FMA50878 and `right optic nerve` FMA50875. There is no
  tibial nerve, no common fibular nerve, no sacral plexus, no gluteal nerve. The subject of an entire
  curriculum entry does not exist. Authored as `concept:sciatic_nerve` and `concept:common_fibular_nerve`.
- **No branch of the internal iliac artery.** The catalog holds 26 arteries; none is a superior gluteal,
  inferior gluteal, internal pudendal, obturator, medial or lateral circumflex femoral, or perforating
  artery. Nor is there a femoral artery or a profunda femoris. So the *entire* named content of the
  `Gluteal vessels` entry is unmodelled and is carried by five concept traces off a real internal iliac
  trunk.
- **No division of the internal iliac artery.** The model is the undivided trunk, so the anterior/posterior
  division split — which is the single commonest short question on this topic — is narrated over a
  highlighted trunk. Recorded in `gaps[]` as the strongest argument for also authoring the diagram mode.
- **No ligament of the pelvis.** Sacrotuberous and sacrospinous absent for the third run running. They
  complete both sciatic foramina; without the sacrospinous ligament the out-through-the-greater,
  in-through-the-lesser course of the internal pudendal artery cannot be shown at all.
- **No skin model**, so the sensory territory of the sciatic nerve — including the saphenous strip on the
  medial leg that proves a lesion is sciatic and not spinal — is narration only.
- **Bone sub-features remain unanchored.** Greater sciatic notch, ischial spine, ischial tuberosity,
  posterior superior iliac spine, greater trochanter, trochanteric fossa, neck of the fibula. Two beats
  turn on them directly (the sciatic surface marking; the artery pinned at the top of the notch) and both
  currently light a whole bone and name the point. The pelvis and fibula meshes were not measured this run;
  an anchor placed by eye beside the hip joint would be worse than none, so none was authored.

**Status — both `candidate`, deliberately, and both stay on the worklist.** The gate passes them, but the
prompt is explicit that a scene which could not be fully resolved is never `ready`, and in both scenes the
structure the curriculum actually named is present only as a concept path. Marking them `ready` would close
two entries whose subjects have no model, and the gap would stop being visible. `candidate` keeps them on
the dev route, keeps them HELD in `sync-state`, and leaves a human one decision to make: *is a trace over
the correct beds, at the correct depth, enough to call the entry taught?* Each scene's `gaps[0]` and
`gaps[1]` state that question in those words.

**Validator** — `validate-scenes.mjs --mark`: **30/30 scenes valid, 0 blocked**, exit 0. Both new scenes
pass all eight stages (schema · canonical · provider-id · existence · ops · capability · purity ·
lifecycle). Every `name` was looked up in `available-meshes.json` and copied character for character; no id
was reused from another scene without checking, and none was invented. Capability note on the sciatic scene
only: `PEEL_LAYER` degrades to a hide of the tagged layer in beat 2, taking the muscle roof off. Every other
op across both scenes is native to the 3D adapter. Purity clean on both: no URL, no file extension, no CDN
host, no library name, no provider name outside `provider{}` and `refs{}`, no attribution string.

**Index** — `build-scene-index.mjs`: index.json written · **30 scenes (19 ready) · 2486 term mappings**
(was 28 / 19 / 2286). Two more scenes, 200 new term mappings, and `ready` unchanged at 19 — which is the

  knee jerk) and the sciatic nerve with its tibial and common fibular divisions (the single fact that
  organises the posterior compartment) are both authored as `concept:` traces — `concept:femoral_nerve`
  and `concept:sciatic_nerve` — over the muscles they supply.
- No femoral artery, femoral vein or popliteal vessels. NAVEL and the popliteal pulse are narrated.
- No tendon separate from its muscle: quadriceps tendon, patellar ligament, the long cord of
  semitendinosus, the membranous tendon of semimembranosus. The patellar ligament is what the reflex
  hammer strikes, so the most-tested single structure in the quadriceps scene has no mesh.
- No bursa (suprapatellar, semimembranosus), no knee capsule, no sacrotuberous ligament, no
  articularis genu.
- Sub-features of whole bones — anterior inferior iliac spine, linea aspera, tibial tuberosity, ischial
  tuberosity, adductor tubercle, head of fibula — are taught by lighting the parent bone and naming the
  feature. Not authored as anchors: the meshes were not measured this run, and an anchor placed by eye is
  worse than none.

**Status decision — why `ready` this time and `candidate` last time.** The gluteal run held its two scenes
at `candidate` because the *subject* of each entry (the sciatic nerve itself, the gluteal arteries
themselves) had no mesh at all — the scene was entirely trace and narration. Here the subject of each entry
is muscle, every muscle exists, and what is missing is the innervation and the tendons. A student can name
the four heads, see which crosses two joints, watch the vasti wrap the shaft in cross-section and identify
the three cords behind their own knee from these scenes. That is the curriculum entry taught. So both are
`ready`, and both `gaps[]` name exactly what the student is not seeing.

## 2026-08-27T03:10Z — run 16 · Thigh · Femoral triangle + Adductor canal

**Sync first.** `sync-state.mjs` ran before anything was read or decided: 207 curriculum structures,
36 covered by 32 scene files, gross 25/81, `next to author: gross / Thigh / Femoral triangle`. STATE.json
was already in step. **No `DEMAND.json` exists**, so this run was cursor-driven, not demand-driven, and took
the next two entries in curriculum order: **Femoral triangle** and **Adductor canal**. Both carry
`preferred_modes: ["3d_anatomy", "diagram"]`; both were authored as `3d_anatomy` with `deferred_modes:
["diagram"]`.

**The prediction from run 15 was overturned on evidence, and this is the one judgement in this run that a
human should look at.** Run 15 closed by flagging these two entries as the gluteal-vessels case — "the
subject of the entry has no mesh… expect `candidate`, not `ready`" — on the premise that there is *no
femoral artery and no femoral vein* in the catalog. That premise is half wrong, and the half that is wrong
is the important half. There is no mesh **named** femoral artery, but `right external iliac artery`
FMA18806 and `right external iliac vein` FMA18885 are the *same tubes*: the external iliac artery becomes
the femoral artery at the inguinal ligament and there is no join, no branch and no anatomical event at the
name change. That is resolution option (b) in the authoring contract — teach it through an adjacent
structure that exists — and it is a much stronger case than a substitution, because the model is not
standing in for the structure, it *is* the structure under its proximal name. Both scenes label it in the
open: "Femoral artery (continuous with the external iliac artery)". So both are `ready`.

The counterargument, recorded because it is real: the modelled length of both vessels lies mostly *above*
the inguinal ligament, so what the student sees inside the triangle and the canal is the top of the vessel
entering, not its full intra-regional course. If a human judges that insufficient, the fix is a one-field
edit to `candidate` in each scene and no other change — the resolution, the naming and the gaps are honest
either way. It was not downgraded here because the subject of both curriculum entries is a **space**, and
the walls, boundaries and floor that define both spaces are completely modelled: base, both borders and all
three floor muscles of the triangle; all three walls and the roof of the canal. A student can name every
boundary, see the apex form where sartorius crosses adductor longus, watch the tunnel open and close in
cross-section, and follow the artery out through the adductor hiatus. That is the curriculum entry taught.

**Models resolved — every id looked up in `available-meshes.json` by the catalog's own `name` and copied
character for character. No id invented, none copied from another scene without checking.**

packed in dorsiflexion, the subtalar joint as the true inverter, the in-front-or-behind-the-axis rule for
every tendon, and the clear space that says whether the talus has shifted. That is most of an MBBS ankle
answer. The ligaments are traced as concepts, named in narration and stated as the first line of `gaps[]`.

syndrome and for every tunnel in the body. Second and cheaper, unchanged: pull femur, patella, tibia,
fibula, talus, calcaneus and hip bone into `meshes-lite/` so the soleal line, the tibial tuberosity, the
malleoli and the trochlear taper can be measured and anchored rather than described — the ankle scene is
now the strongest case in the corpus for measured landmark anchors, because its whole mechanism is a shape
difference of a few millimetres between the front and the back of one articular surface. The SVG artwork
commission still outranks all of it, because it alone unblocks seven of the eleven held structures.

alternative reading — hold at `candidate` — would leave the topic's hardest region invisible to students in

## Run 21 — 2026-08-27

**Mode: cursor-driven.** `DEMAND.json` does not exist, so there was nothing to prioritise from and the run
fell back to the cursor as instructed. `sync-state.mjs` ran first, before anything was read or decided, and
reported STATE.json already in step: 46 covered by 42 files, `next to author: gross / Thoracic Wall &
Diaphragm / Diaphragm`. Attempted the next two at the cursor: **Diaphragm** and **Intercostal neurovascular
bundle**.

**Diaphragm — `ready`.** 25 structures (4 parts), 9 views, 38 ops.

Models resolved: `diaphragm` FMA13295; openings by transmitted structure `inferior vena cava` FMA10951,
`esophagus` FMA7131, `descending aorta` FMA3784; levels FMA9991 / FMA10037 / FMA10081 / FMA13072; arcuate
proxies FMA22342, FMA22343, FMA22348, FMA22349; origin FMA8531, FMA8532, FMA8533, FMA8534, FMA7488, BP28,
BP24; above FMA7337, FMA7371, FMA7274; below FMA7197, FMA7148, FMA7196.

Models NOT found — searched the full catalog by name, nothing returned: **central tendon, right crus, left
crus, crura, median / medial / lateral arcuate ligament, phrenic nerve, pericardium**. Not one of these
exists at any spelling. So the muscle's own architecture is unshowable and option (b) from the authoring
rule was used instead — teach each opening through the structure that passes through it. That carries the
levels and the contents correctly and carries nothing about the tissue each opening is cut in; `gaps[]`
says so in those words rather than implying the scene is complete.

**Intercostal neurovascular bundle — `candidate`.** 12 structures (7 parts), 7 views, 29 ops.

Models NOT found: **intercostal vein, intercostal artery, intercostal nerve** at any level, plus **azygos
vein, hemiazygos, internal thoracic artery, parietal pleura**. A sweep of all 934 names for *artery*, *vein*
and *nerve* returns 49 entries, none of them intercostal. The entire subject of the scene is unmodelled —
the purest form of the problem the last three runs have been logging.

The previous run's note offered two ways out, a `concept:` trace or `planned`. Both were worse than a third:
**anchors on a measured groove**. `tools/derive-costal-groove.mjs` was written this run to place them.

**The tool caught two of its own errors before they reached a scene, which is the only reason this entry is
worth reading.**

1. *The frame assertion was wrong and failed loudly.* It asserted Z must be a rib's smallest span, on the
   assumption that a rib is a flat hoop in a near-transverse plane. Rib 5 measures 112 × 183 × **122** mm —
   a rib descends about as far as it is wide. The assertion was wrong, not the mesh. It mattered because
   the same false picture was about to be used to find the groove. Replaced with three claims that are
   actually diagnostic of the LPS frame: right ribs entirely at X<0, rib 6 centroid below rib 5, and the
   rib's head end clearly medial to its most lateral point.
2. *The detrending was fitting noise, and the comment defending it was overclaiming.* Z was being regressed
   on theta **inside** the ±2.5° slab. A slab that narrow is a compact blob, not a strip, so the regression
   has almost no leverage and fits the rib's own thickness: across slab widths of 1.5 / 2.5 / 4 / 6° the
   fitted slope came out **-1.94, -0.42, -0.28, -0.55 mm/deg — it changed sign** — and moved the chosen
   point by up to 7.7 mm. Fitted on a wide ±15° window instead, the same slope is **-0.42, -0.34, -0.30,
   -0.37** across four window sizes. The header comment had also claimed a 0.8 mm/deg descent and that a raw
   minimum-Z would land "at the far end of the slab, every time"; the real descent is 0.35 mm/deg, giving
   1.7 mm of tilt across a slab 19 mm tall, so detrending is a *modest* correction that buys insensitivity
   to slab width and not much else. The comment now says that instead of the dramatic version.

Also switched the measurement from `meshes-lite` to `meshes`: at ±2.5° the full rib gives 133 vertices in
the slab and the lite rib gives 15, and at 15 the answer is quantised to whatever the decimator kept.

Final anchors, mid-axillary station: `ic_vein` uvw [0.037, 0.538, 0.389] on rib 5 — measured; `ic_artery`
[0.037, 0.538, 0.373] and `ic_nerve` [0.037, 0.538, 0.357] — the same groove point dropped 2 mm and 4 mm,
**estimated**, because no measurement of a bone can say where the vein ends and the artery begins;
`collateral` uvw [0.068, 0.518, 0.477] on rib 6 — measured. Reported stability: 2.0 mm on rib 5 and 3.5 mm
on rib 6 across slab widths 1.5–6°. All four `status:"needs-review"`, each `calibrated_by` stating whether
it is a measurement or an estimate.

The scene is `candidate` on purpose. It validates clean and would be accepted as `ready`, but all three
structures the student is there to learn are authored markers, and marking that `ready` would retire the
gap. It appears in HELD, which is where it belongs.

**Validator — `validate-scenes.mjs --mark`: 44/44 valid, 0 blocked.** Both new scenes passed all eight
stages on the first attempt. Diaphragm degrades `PEEL_LAYER` only; the bundle scene degrades nothing. The
anchor lifecycle rule behaved as designed — `needs-review` anchors pass without a `reviewed_by`, and
`calibrated_by` is present on all four so no warning fired. Standing warning unchanged: the seven `planned`
SVG scenes still emit "unknown to the svg adapter" for every op because `CAPABILITIES.svg` is still
`{ native: [], degraded: [] }`.

**Index — `build-scene-index.mjs`: 44 scenes (32 ready) · 3692 term mappings**, up from 42/31/3543. The 149
new mappings are the diaphragm and the bundle: central tendon, crus and crura, the three arcuate ligaments,
caval opening and vena caval foramen, oesophageal and aortic hiatus, T8 / T10 / T12, phrenic nerve and
C3 C4 C5, shoulder-tip pain and Kehr sign, hiatus / Bochdalek / Morgagni hernia, eventration, hiccup,
costodiaphragmatic recess; then VAN, costal groove, collateral branch, lateral and anterior cutaneous

**State** — `sync-state.mjs` re-run after writing: **48 structures covered by 44 scene files; gross 36/81**.
`next to author: gross / Thoracic Wall & Diaphragm / Pleura & pleural cavity`. STATE.json reconciled from
