**HELD — 12 structures, up one.** The new entry is this run's own `Intercostal neurovascular bundle`, added
deliberately. The other eleven are unchanged for a ninth run: seven `planned` diagram scenes waiting on SVG
artwork and a real `CAPABILITIES.svg`, four `candidate` 3D scenes waiting on one human judgement each.

**The rule the last run asked for, now that it has been tested against a harder case.** Run 20 proposed
writing down the release rule it had applied twice: author `ready` on what the walls and relationships
genuinely teach, list every absent content in `gaps[]`, never claim a neighbouring curriculum entry. This
run applied it to the diaphragm and it held. It then met a case it does **not** cover, and the distinction
is worth stating: the popliteal fossa is a *space* whose walls are modelled, so the walls teach the space.
The intercostal bundle is not a space — it is *only* contents, and there is no wall that teaches it. The
rule needs a second clause: **when the subject itself is unmodelled, adjacency is not enough and the scene
stays `candidate` however well it validates.** That is the line between the diaphragm scene going out
`ready` and the bundle scene being held, and both went through the same gate.

**Note for the next run.** Cursor is on **Pleura & pleural cavity**, the last entry in this topic, then
**Heart & Pericardium** — where `Heart chambers` and `Conducting system` are still uncovered and the
existing heart scene explicitly refuses to claim chambers.
- `Pleura & pleural cavity` has **no mesh of any kind** — logged three runs ago and confirmed again in this
  run's sweep. There is no parietal pleura, no visceral pleura, no pleural cavity. It is a two-layer sac
  and a potential space, so there is nothing to resolve and nothing adjacent that teaches it. On this run's
  new second clause it cannot be `ready`. The lung lobes and the innermost intercostal bound the space and
  would make an honest `candidate`; `planned` pending SVG is the other defensible call. Either way, decide
  it on the rule and not by feel.
- The **anchor route is now proven** and is available wherever a structure is unmodelled but sits on
  something that is. `tools/derive-costal-groove.mjs` is the template: measure on the full-resolution mesh,
  fit any trend on a window wide enough to determine it, report sensitivity to every arbitrary knob, and
  mark estimates as estimates in `calibrated_by`. It is worth reaching for before reaching for `planned`.

**Still waiting on a networked job.** Unchanged and twenty-one runs old. **Nerve meshes** and **vein meshes**
remain the highest-value acquisition and this run is the strongest evidence yet: two consecutive structures
were degraded by their absence, and one of them could not be released at all. **Pleura and serous membrane
sets** now rank alongside the intercostal membranes, because the very next structure needs them. The **SVG
artwork commission** still unblocks seven of the twelve held structures and probably an eighth in Pleura.

## Run 22 — 2026-08-27

**Mode: cursor-driven.** `DEMAND.json` does not exist in `viz-training/`, so there was nothing to prioritise
from and the run fell back to the cursor as instructed. `sync-state.mjs` ran first, before anything else was
read or decided, and reported STATE.json already in step: **48 covered by 44 files**, `next to author:
gross / Thoracic Wall & Diaphragm / Pleura & pleural cavity`. Attempted the next two at the cursor:
**Pleura & pleural cavity** and **Heart chambers** (the next uncovered entry, since `Heart (external)`,
`Cardiac cycle`, `Coronary arteries & cardiac veins` and `Heart valves` are all covered by the existing
heart scene).

### Pleura & pleural cavity — `planned`, mode `diagram`

22 structures (17 parts), 7 views, 33 ops. `deferred_modes: ["3d_anatomy"]`.

**Models resolved: none, deliberately — this is an SVG scene and carries no refs.**

**Models NOT found.** Swept all 934 catalog names for *pleura*, *serous*, *serosa*, *membrane*, *cavity*,
*endothoracic*, *fascia* and *pericard*. The complete return is four interosseous membranes, two tensor
fasciae latae and the septum pellucidum. There is **no parietal pleura, no visceral pleura, no pleural
cavity, no endothoracic fascia and no suprapleural membrane** at any spelling. This is the fourth
consecutive run to log the pleura as absent and the third to log the pericardium; the pattern is now clear
enough to name: **the free set is built from solid organs, muscles, bones and vessels, and serous membranes
are not in it.** No further ingest of the same source will close this. It needs a different provider.

**The decision run 21 asked to be made on the rule and not by feel.** Run 21 left two defensible calls open
— a `candidate` 3D scene bounded by the lung lobes and the innermost intercostal, or `planned` pending SVG.
Took `planned` / `diagram`, for a reason that follows directly from run 21's own second clause: *when the
subject itself is unmodelled, adjacency is not enough and the scene stays `candidate` however well it
validates.* A 3D pleura scene therefore could not have been released either. Given two routes that both
withhold the scene from students today, the one that ends in a **complete** teaching artefact is better than
the one that ends in ribs and lungs with the subject missing from between them. The lung, chest wall,
diaphragm and mediastinum are authored as diagram context; all four exist in the catalog and become real
refs the day a pleural sac model does, which is recorded in `gaps[]` so the 3D route stays open.

**Curriculum intent preserved.** `preferred_modes` in CURRICULUM.json is untouched, `3d_anatomy` remains
first, and `deferred_modes: ["3d_anatomy"]` carries it on the scene.

### Heart chambers — `planned`, mode `diagram`

23 structures (16 parts), 7 views, 33 ops. `deferred_modes: ["3d_anatomy"]`.

**Models NOT found.** Swept for *atrium*, *ventricle*, *chamber*, *septum*, *auricle*, *cusp* and
*foramen ovale*. The return is the four ventricles of the **brain** (FMA78469, FMA78450, FMA78449,
FMA78454), the septum pellucidum, the three papillary muscles, the interventricular foramen of the brain
and `wall of heart` FMA7274. **Nothing cardiac that is a chamber, and no aortic valve** (FMA7236 confirmed
absent, as the heart scene's `gaps[]` has said since 2026-08-23).

**Why this is not a second heart scene.** `gross__heart-pericardium__heart` covers four entries in this
topic and explicitly refuses `Heart chambers` because it teaches chambers through their valves. Building a
3D scene here from the same tricuspid, mitral, pulmonary valve and papillary muscles would have produced a
duplicate that claimed the entry without teaching it — precisely the failure the authoring rule calls worse
than never authoring it, because the gap would stop being visible. The diagram engine can draw a cut heart
with crista terminalis, fossa ovalis, moderator band, infundibulum, aortic vestibule and both septa, which
is the whole of what the curriculum note asks for (*4 chambers + septa + valves in cut view*).

**The gap is closable and says exactly how.** FMA7096 right atrium, FMA7097 left atrium, FMA7098 right
ventricle, FMA7101 left ventricle, FMA7236 aortic valve, FMA7133 interventricular septum are in the full
archive. `tools/ingest-full-archive.mjs --fetch` on those six, then re-author this scene as `3d_anatomy` —
the structure list, the seven beats and the narration survive the move unchanged.

### Gate

**Validator — `validate-scenes.mjs --mark`: 46/46 scenes valid, 0 blocked.** Both new scenes passed all
eight stages on the first run. No scene was marked `blocked`; nothing was left unfixed and nothing was
advanced past silently. Warnings only, and only from stage 6: with `CAPABILITIES.svg` still `{native: [],
degraded: []}`, every op in an SVG scene reports *unknown to the svg adapter — it will be ignored, not
degraded*. That is 33 warnings per scene and it is the same noise the seven earlier `planned` scenes
produce. It is not a scene defect: it is `CAPABILITIES.svg` being an empty placeholder. Worth filling in
when the SVG engine's real op support is known, because at nine planned scenes the warning volume now
buries anything genuine that stage 6 might have to say.

**Index — `build-scene-index.mjs`: 46 scenes (32 ready) · 3881 term mappings**, up from 44/32/3692. The 189
new mappings are the two scenes' vocabulary: visceral and parietal pleura, costal / diaphragmatic /
mediastinal / cervical, cupola and Sibson's fascia, potential space, negative intrapleural pressure,
costodiaphragmatic recess and costophrenic angle, line of pleural reflection, 8-10-12, pulmonary ligament,
pleurisy and referred shoulder-tip pain, pneumothorax, tension pneumothorax, effusion, triangle of safety,
thoracocentesis; then crista terminalis, musculi pectinati, fossa ovalis and patent foramen ovale,
Eustachian and Thebesian valves, trabeculae carneae, moderator band and septomarginal trabecula,
infundibulum and conus arteriosus, aortic vestibule, membranous septum, VSD and ASD, tetralogy of Fallot.

**State — `sync-state.mjs` re-run after writing: 50 structures covered by 46 scene files; gross 36/81.**
`next to author: gross / Heart & Pericardium / Conducting system`. STATE.json reconciled from the scenes
directory; nothing hand-edited. Note that `gross 36/81` is unchanged from run 21 — the bar counts `ready`,
and both of this run's scenes are `planned`, so they show in HELD instead.

**HELD — 14 structures, up two, both this run's own.** Nine `planned` diagram scenes waiting on SVG artwork
and a real `CAPABILITIES.svg`; four `candidate` 3D scenes waiting on one human judgement each; one
`candidate` awaiting anchor review.

### What this run learned

**Thoracic Wall & Diaphragm is now fully authored, 5 of 5 — and only 3 of the 5 are `ready`.** The topic is
a fair sample of the corpus's real constraint: the bones and muscles resolved outright, and everything that
is a membrane, a nerve or a vessel did not. Two of the five are held for the same missing-mesh reason.

**A third route now has a name.** Run 21 established anchors as the route when a structure is unmodelled but
*sits on something that is* — the bundle in its costal groove. This run met the case where the structure
sits on nothing: the pleura is a sac, the chambers are voids. **Anchors cannot mark a void; only a drawing
can.** So the order to try is: resolve the model, then a more specific sibling, then a measured anchor on a
parent, then the diagram engine. `planned`/`diagram` is not the failure case — it is the correct route for
subjects that have no surface to hang a marker on.

### Still waiting

**SVG artwork is now the single biggest blocker in the corpus** — nine `planned` scenes, up from seven, all
fully authored with structures, beats, ops and narration, and none of them renders a pixel today. Two of the
nine are cardiac and thoracic core material. `CAPABILITIES.svg` needs filling in at the same time.

**Nerve and vein meshes** remain the highest-value acquisition, twenty-two runs old and unchanged.
**Serous membrane sets** (pleura, pericardium, peritoneum) now rank alongside them, and the peritoneum will
be needed by the abdomen topics shortly. **The four-chamber ingest is the cheapest win on the list**: six
known ids from an archive already integrated, one script already written, and it would convert this run's
heart chambers scene from `planned` to a `ready` 3D scene and close the oldest declared gap in the corpus.


## Run 23 — 2026-08-27

**Cursor-driven, not demand-driven.** `DEMAND.json` does not exist in the repo, so there was no evidence
list to outrank the curriculum and the run fell back to the cursor as instructed. Structures attempted:
**gross / Heart & Pericardium / Conducting system** and **gross / Lungs & Mediastinum / Lungs** — the
last entry of one topic and the first of the next.

**sync-state first, before anything else.** It reported 50 structures covered by 46 scene files, gross
36/81, 14 held, `next to author: gross / Heart & Pericardium / Conducting system`. STATE.json was already
in step, so nothing was rewritten on the way in. Both target structures were absent from
`coveredStructures`, confirmed against the printed table rather than remembered.

### Conducting system — `diagram`, `planned`

`preferred_modes` is `["diagram","3d_anatomy"]`, so `diagram` is the **first** renderable mode, not a
fallback. `3d_anatomy` recorded in `deferred_modes`.

**Models resolved: none, and none were sought.** A diagram scene carries no refs, and the resolution
search was run anyway to record what exists: all 934 catalog entries matched against *node*, *bundle*,
*Purkinje*, *sinoatrial*, *atrioventricular*, *conducting* — **zero cardiac hits.** The nearest entries are
the three papillary muscles and `wall of heart`.

**This is a different gap from the chambers.** Run 22 could name six ids in the full BodyParts3D archive
that would convert its scene to 3D. There is no equivalent list here. The conducting system is millimetres
of specialised muscle indistinguishable from surrounding myocardium in a segmented surface model — it is
not under-fetched, it is unsegmentable at this provider. The gap says so plainly rather than leaving a
future run to search for it again.

17 structures (13 parts), 7 views, 38 ops.

### Lungs — `3d_anatomy`, `ready`

**Every lobe resolved on the first lookup**, which has not happened for a viscus before: `FMA7333`,
`FMA7383`, `FMA7337`, `FMA7370`, `FMA7371`. Seven context models resolved too — trachea, bronchus,
pulmonary artery, pulmonary vein, wall of heart, diaphragm, esophagus.

**Models NOT found:** no pleura (its own scene, its own gap); no separate right and left principal
bronchi — the catalog holds one generic `bronchus`, so the right-versus-left aspiration difference is
carried by narration; no bronchopulmonary segments; no phrenic or vagus nerve; no azygos vein.

**New tool: `tools/derive-lung-landmarks.mjs`.** Nine anchors measured on the meshes, not estimated. It
asserts the LPS frame on the meshes it is handed — right lobes at X&lt;0, left lobes at X&gt;0, right upper
lobe above right lower lobe, heart anterior to the lower lobe — and exits without emitting if any assertion
fails. Contacts printed with their gap: oblique fissure right 0.10 mm, left 0.13 mm, cardiac impression
0.61 mm, right hilum 0.73 mm, left hilum 0.81 mm. Extremes printed with their distance from the lobe
centroid.

**One anchor was refused.** The horizontal fissure needs the upper-to-middle-lobe contact, and `FMA7383`
is in the catalog but not in `meshes/` or `meshes-lite/`. The tool never fetches, so the landmark was left
unauthored and recorded in `gaps[]` rather than guessed. An anchor placed by eye is worse than a missing
one: it puts the words *horizontal fissure* somewhere plausible and the student believes it.

21 structures (14 parts), 7 views, 41 ops.

### Gate results

**`validate-scenes.mjs --mark`: 48/48 valid, 0 blocked.** Both new scenes passed all eight stages.
`gross__lungs-mediastinum__lungs` → **ready** [degrades: PEEL_LAYER].
`gross__heart-pericardium__conducting-system` → **planned**, with the usual 38 capability warnings from an
empty `CAPABILITIES.svg`. No errors at schema, canonical, provider-id, existence, ops, purity or lifecycle.

**Index — `build-scene-index.mjs`: 48 scenes (33 ready) · 4091 term mappings**, up from 46/32/3881. The 210
new mappings are the two vocabularies: sinoatrial and atrioventricular node, triangle of Koch, tendon of
Todaro, bundle of His, internodal pathways, Bachmann's bundle, moderator band and septomarginal trabecula,
Purkinje fibres, P wave / PR interval / QRS, first, second and complete heart block, Stokes-Adams,
bundle branch block, hemiblock, SA and AV nodal arteries, RALS; then oblique and horizontal fissure,
lingula, cardiac notch, hilum and root of the lung, pulmonary ligament, eparterial bronchus,
bronchopulmonary segment, costodiaphragmatic recess, Sibson's fascia, Pancoast tumour, azygos lobe.

**State — `sync-state.mjs` re-run after writing: 52 structures covered by 48 scene files; gross 37/81.**
`next to author: gross / Lungs & Mediastinum / Tracheobronchial tree`. Nothing hand-edited.

**HELD — 15 structures, up one.** Ten `planned` diagram scenes waiting on SVG artwork and a real
`CAPABILITIES.svg`; five `candidate` scenes waiting on one human judgement each.

### What this run learned

**Two gaps that look identical are not.** Both the chambers and the conducting system are cardiac, both
went to `diagram`, both are `planned`. But the chambers gap is a fetch — six known ids, one script, an
afternoon. The conducting system gap is a provider limit with no known closure. Writing both as *no mesh
found* would have flattened a solvable problem into an unsolvable-looking one, and the six-id list would
have stopped being visible. A gap is only useful if it says what would close it, or says plainly that
nothing would.

**The organ topics may run easier than the limb topics did.** The lungs resolved whole, with lobes as the
catalog's natural unit — no hunting for sibling heads, no teaching-through-a-neighbour. Liver, kidney,
spleen and stomach are all likely to behave the same way. The limbs were hard because the interesting
structures there are nerves and vessels; the abdomen's interesting structures are organs, and organs is
what a surface-model catalog is good at.

### Still waiting

**SVG artwork remains the single biggest blocker** — ten `planned` scenes now, fully authored and rendering
nothing. **The four-chamber ingest is still the cheapest win**: six ids, one script already written, would
convert run 22's scene from `planned` to `ready` 3D. **`FMA7383` should be added to the local mesh set** at
the same time — one file closes the horizontal fissure anchor. **Nerve and vein meshes** remain the highest
-value acquisition, twenty-three runs old and unchanged.

## Run 24 — 2026-08-27

**Not demand-driven.** `DEMAND.json` does not exist in the repo, so there was no evidence to outrank the
curriculum and the run fell back to the cursor. `sync-state.mjs` ran first, before anything was read or
decided, and reported 52 structures covered by 48 scene files with `next to author: gross / Lungs &
Mediastinum / Tracheobronchial tree`. STATE.json was already in step.

**Attempted: `Tracheobronchial tree`, then `Mediastinum`.** Both `preferred_modes` `["3d_anatomy",
"diagram"]`, both authored as `3d_anatomy` with `diagram` recorded in `deferred_modes`. Both passed the
gate and are `ready`.

### Tracheobronchial tree — and Trachea, which came with it

`FMA7394` trachea and `FMA7409` bronchus resolved by name from the catalog. Context: manubrium `FMA7486`,
body of sternum `FMA7487`, T4 `FMA9248`, arch of aorta `FMA3768`, and all five lung lobes.

**A third curriculum entry is covered, deliberately.** "Trachea" sits two places further along the same
topic with views `location` and `cross_section` and the note *C-rings; bifurcation*. Every one of those is
in this scene already — the trachea mesh is its subject, beat 2 gives C6 and the lower border of T4, beat 3
cuts across it for the C-rings. A separate scene would have been the same mesh, the same two levels and the
same cut, authored twice. So `covers` names both, and `sync-state.mjs` accepted both spellings against the
curriculum. Coverage moved 37 → 40 in gross on two authored scenes.

**Models NOT found:** no right or left principal bronchus separately — the catalog holds one generic
`bronchus`, so *wider, shorter, more vertical* is carried by narration and by highlighting the right lower
lobe rather than by two comparable meshes. No lobar bronchi, no segmental bronchi, no bronchopulmonary
segments. Nothing below the segmental bronchus exists in a whole-body surface catalog: no bronchioles, no
alveolar ducts, no alveoli, so beat 5 ends inside the lobe. No larynx and no cricoid cartilage as part of
the airway — only `thyroid cartilage` exists, and it is not modelled continuous with the trachea.

16 structures (7 parts), 7 views, 36 ops.

### Mediastinum

Eleven contents resolved outright — heart wall, ascending aorta, arch, descending aorta, both venae cavae,
pulmonary artery, right brachiocephalic vein, right subclavian artery, trachea, bronchus — plus manubrium,
sternal body, T4, T12 and the two upper lobes as boundaries.

**Models NOT found:** **no pericardium.** That is the important one: the pericardium is what divides the
inferior mediastinum into anterior, middle and posterior, so this scene draws three of its four divisions
from the heart's own surface and says so in `gaps[]`. No thoracic nerves at all — phrenic, vagus, both
recurrent laryngeals, sympathetic chain, splanchnics — so beat 7 traces
`concept:left-recurrent-laryngeal-nerve` along the structures it passes instead of showing a nerve. No
azygos vein and no thoracic duct, though both are named repeatedly. No mediastinal lymph nodes anywhere in
the catalog. `FMA7131` oesophagus, `FMA71194`/`FMA71195` thymus lobes and `FMA13295` diaphragm are all in
the catalog but were not in the local mesh set, so nothing could be measured against them.

**"Great vessels" was NOT marked covered.** Every great vessel is in this scene, and it would have been one
line in `covers` to claim it. It is a separate curriculum entry with views `vasculature` and
`associated_organs`, and this scene teaches none of the branching pattern, surface marking or development
that entry wants — it uses the vessels to define divisions. Claiming it would have removed it from the
worklist and nobody would have come back. It is now the next structure at the cursor.

25 structures (15 parts), 8 views, 48 ops.

### New tool: `tools/derive-mediastinum-landmarks.mjs`

Fifteen anchors across the two scenes, fourteen of them measured. The tool re-proves the LPS frame on the
meshes it is handed before emitting anything — manubrium above the sternal body, sternum anterior to T4,
ascending aorta to the body-right of the descending, trachea above the heart — and exits without output if
any assertion fails. Contacts printed with their gap: sternal angle 0.11 mm, cavo-atrial junction 0.13 mm,
IVC-atrial 0.34 mm, arch over the airway 0.36 mm, aortic hiatus 0.95 mm, ligamentum arteriosum 1.93 mm,
posterior end of the transthoracic plane 3.72 mm, trachea to T4 11.41 mm.

**A measured number is not automatically a true one.** The first version of the tool derived the aortic
hiatus as the lowest vertex of the descending aorta. It ran, it emitted a clean `uvw`, and it was wrong:
`FMA3784` continues past the diaphragm to the aortic bifurcation, so its floor sits at z 960 while T12
bottoms out at 1063. The lowest point of that mesh is in the abdomen, and an anchor there would have
labelled the bifurcation "aortic hiatus" with a measurement to vouch for it — worse than a hand-placed
anchor, because it carries evidence. It was caught by checking the mesh's z-extent against T12 rather than
by trusting the tool's own output, and re-derived as a contact with T12. Geometry answers the question you
actually asked; it does not check that the question was the right one.

**The two large gaps were left visible rather than closed by guessing.** The trachea-to-T4 contact is
11.41 mm and stayed in `calibrated_by` unrounded, because that gap is the oesophagus and saying so teaches
something. The oesophagus anchor in the mediastinum scene is the one landmark in either scene that was not
measured — the mesh was not local — so it is recorded as `author:` rather than `geometry:` and named in
`gaps[]` as the thing to replace first.

### Gate results

**`validate-scenes.mjs --mark`: 50/50 valid, 0 blocked.** Both new scenes passed all eight stages —
schema, canonical, provider-id, existence, ops, capability, purity, lifecycle. Both `ready`
[degrades: PEEL_LAYER]. No stage produced an error on either file.

**Index — `build-scene-index.mjs`: 50 scenes (35 ready) · 4294 term mappings**, up from 48/33/4091. The 203
new mappings are the airway and mediastinal vocabularies: carina, bifurcation of the trachea, C-shaped
rings, trachealis, tracheostomy, eparterial and hyparterial bronchus, segmental bronchus, bronchopulmonary
segment, terminal and respiratory bronchiole, anatomical dead space, twenty-three generations, right main
bronchus intubation, widened carina, subcarinal nodes, postural drainage; then plane of Ludwig,
transthoracic plane, the four divisions, thymoma and the four Ts, ligamentum arteriosum, ductus arteriosus,
left recurrent laryngeal nerve, cavo-atrial junction, SVC obstruction, chylothorax, thoracic duct,
mediastinal shift, aortic hiatus, oesophageal hiatus, caval opening, T8/T10/T12.

**State — `sync-state.mjs` re-run after writing: 55 structures covered by 50 scene files; gross 40/81.**
`next to author: gross / Lungs & Mediastinum / Great vessels`. Nothing hand-edited.

**HELD — 15 structures, unchanged.** Ten `planned` diagram scenes waiting on SVG artwork and a real
`CAPABILITIES.svg`; five `candidate` scenes waiting on one human judgement each. No new blocks.

### What this run learned

**A combined scene is only honest when the second entry gets everything it was promised.** "Trachea" was
covered here because its two curriculum views and its whole note fit inside beats 2 and 3 with the mesh
already loaded. "Great vessels" was refused in the other scene for the mirror-image reason: the meshes were
all there, but the teaching that entry asks for was not. The test is never *is the model in the scene* —
it is *would a student who only opened this scene be able to do what the entry says*. Both answers came out
of the same rule and they came out opposite ways.

**Derivation tools need a sanity check the tool cannot perform.** Twenty-four runs of measuring landmarks
have made `geometry:` feel like a guarantee, and this run it nearly was not one. The frame assertions
caught nothing because the frame was fine; the error was in choosing *lowest vertex* as the definition of a
landmark on a mesh that extends past the region being taught. Every future derive tool should print the
mesh's extent alongside its landmarks, so the author sees when a mesh reaches beyond the scene.

### Still waiting

**A pericardium mesh is now the highest-value single acquisition for the thorax** — it defines three of the
four mediastinal divisions and neither the mediastinum scene nor the heart scene can draw its own
boundaries without it. **SVG artwork remains the single biggest blocker overall**, ten `planned` scenes
authored and rendering nothing. **The four-chamber ingest** is still six ids and a script already written.
**Local ingest of `FMA7131`, `FMA71194`, `FMA71195`, `FMA13295` and `FMA7383`** would close five named
gaps across three thoracic scenes at once — all five are in the catalog, none is in `meshes/`. **Nerve and
vein meshes** remain the highest-value acquisition overall, twenty-four runs old and unchanged.

### Great vessels — the entry run 24 refused to claim

Run 24 wrote the Mediastinum scene with every great vessel already loaded and deliberately did not mark
this entry covered, on the grounds that a scene using vessels to define divisions does not teach the
vessels. That decision is now paid off rather than merely recorded: this scene shares eleven meshes with
the mediastinum scene and teaches almost nothing the same way.

**Models resolved — 21 ids, every one checked against `available-meshes.json` by catalog `name`, none
copied from another scene without re-checking, none invented.**

Vessels (13): `FMA3736` ascending aorta · `FMA3768` arch of aorta · `FMA3784` descending aorta ·
`FMA66326` pulmonary artery · `FMA66643` pulmonary vein · `FMA4720` superior vena cava · `FMA10951`
inferior vena cava · `FMA4751` right brachiocephalic vein · `FMA4761` left brachiocephalic vein ·
`FMA4058` left common carotid artery · `FMA4694` left subclavian artery · `FMA3953` right subclavian
artery · `FMA3941` right common carotid artery. Context (8): `FMA7274` wall of heart · `FMA7246` pulmonary
valve · `FMA7394` trachea · `FMA7486` manubrium · `FMA7487` body of sternum · `FMA9248` T4 · `FMA10081`
T12 · `FMA7333`/`FMA7370` upper lobes.

**Models NOT found — recorded in `gaps[]`, not worked around silently.**
- **No brachiocephalic trunk.** The largest branch of the arch, and the vessel the entire left-versus-right
  asymmetry of the neck depends on. Taught by option (b), through the two vessels it divides into — right
  common carotid and right subclavian, both of which exist — plus a measured anchor at its origin.
- **No pulmonary trunk.** One generic `pulmonary artery` mesh covers the trunk and both branches, so the
  right pulmonary artery's course behind the ascending aorta and the SVC is narration.
- **No separate pulmonary veins.** One generic mesh, so "four, two from each lung" cannot be shown.
- **No ligamentum arteriosum, no ductus arteriosus, no aortic valve, no azygos vein, no thoracic nerve of
  any kind.** Patent ductus and the hoarseness of a left hilar tumour have no model at all.

34 structures (25 parts), 8 views, 53 ops.

### Rectus abdominis

**Models resolved — 17 ids.** Parts (5): `FMA13377`/`FMA13378` right and left rectus abdominis ·
`FMA11336` linea alba · `FMA22346`/`FMA22347` pyramidalis. Context (12): `FMA13336`/`FMA13337` external
oblique · `FMA13892` right internal oblique · `FMA22344` right transversus abdominis ·
`FMA8070`/`FMA8194`/`FMA8248` fifth, sixth and seventh right costal cartilages · `FMA7488` xiphoid
process · `FMA16586`/`FMA16587` hip bones · `FMA21964` right inguinal ligament · `FMA7487` sternal body.

**Models NOT found:** no tendinous intersection, no arcuate line, no rectus sheath, no umbilicus, no
thoracoabdominal nerve, no superior or inferior epigastric artery. The first three are not absences a
provider can fix — a tendinous intersection is a marking on a muscle and an arcuate line is an edge of a
fascia, so neither will ever be a mesh. That distinction is now written into `gaps[]` explicitly, because
"the catalog lacks it" and "no surface catalog can ever carry it" are different notes to a maintainer.

**Four adjacent curriculum entries were NOT marked covered:** `Rectus sheath`, `Flat abdominal muscles`,
`Inguinal canal`, `Inguinal ligament & landmarks`. All four have meshes present in this scene as context,
and the sheath rule is even stated in beat 4 because the muscle is unintelligible without it. Stating a
rule is not teaching an entry: the arcuate line, the layers above and below it, the transversalis fascia
and the deep ring are all untaught here. Same test as run 24 applied to a scene four times as tempting.

25 structures (13 parts), 7 views, 36 ops.

### Two new tools

**`tools/derive-great-vessel-landmarks.mjs`** — twelve anchors, all measured, on fifteen local meshes.
Frame re-proved before anything is emitted: manubrium above sternal body, sternum anterior to T4, ascending
aorta body-right of descending, arch above the heart.

**`tools/derive-abdominal-wall-landmarks.mjs`** — eight anchors, all measured, on seven local meshes.
Frame re-proved: sternum above hip bone, rectus anterior to L1, right rectus body-right of the midline
sternum.

**Four anchors reproduce run 24's values to three decimal places.** The great-vessel tool and the
mediastinum tool were written independently against the same meshes and agree exactly on the sternal angle
`[0.499, 0.011, 0.128]` on the manubrium, the aortic hiatus `[0.113, 0.429, 0.45]` on the descending aorta,
the cavo-atrial junction `[0.64, 0.055, 0.124]` on the SVC, and the IVC opening `[0.965, 0.606, 0.986]` on
the IVC. Nothing was copied between them.

A fifth pair agrees on the measurement but not on the anchor, and the difference is worth stating rather
than counting as a match: run 24's `ligamentum_arteriosum` and this run's `pulmonary_bifurcation` are the
same arch-to-pulmonary-artery contact at the same 1.93 mm gap, but one is expressed as a `uvw` on the arch
and the other as a `uvw` on the pulmonary artery, so the two coordinate triples are unrelated by
construction. Same measurement, different parent — checked, not assumed, and the first draft of this entry
claimed five matches before the comparison was actually run.

### Gate results

**`validate-scenes.mjs --mark`: 52/52 valid, 0 blocked.** Both new scenes passed all eight stages —
schema, canonical, provider-id, existence, ops, capability, purity, lifecycle. Both `ready`
[degrades: PEEL_LAYER]. No stage produced an error on either file.

**Index — `build-scene-index.mjs`: 52 scenes (37 ready) · 4585 term mappings**, up from 50/35/4294. The 291
new mappings are the great-vessel and abdominal-wall vocabularies: aortic root, sinuses of Valsalva,
sinotubular junction, aortic knuckle, brachiocephalic trunk, innominate artery, aortic isthmus, traumatic
aortic transection, widened mediastinum, coarctation, rib notching, radiofemoral delay, saddle embolus,
pulmonary vein isolation, cavo-atrial junction, central line tip, venous angle; then linea alba, linea
semilunaris, tendinous intersections, arcuate line, pyramidalis, rectus sheath haematoma, Carnett's sign,
divarication of the recti, epigastric hernia, Spigelian hernia, midline, paramedian and Pfannenstiel
incisions, TAP block, thoracoabdominal nerves T7–T12.

**State — `sync-state.mjs` re-run after writing: 57 structures covered by 52 scene files; gross 42/81.**
`next to author: gross / Anterior Abdominal Wall & Inguinal Region / Flat abdominal muscles`. Nothing
hand-edited.

**HELD — 15 structures, unchanged.** Ten `planned` diagram scenes waiting on SVG artwork and a real
`CAPABILITIES.svg`; five `candidate` scenes waiting on one human judgement each. No new blocks.

### What this run learned

**A gap can be a measurement.** The brachiocephalic trunk is missing from the catalog, so the anchor at its
origin was derived as the nearest contact between the arch and the right subclavian artery — the two
vessels it runs between. The tool printed 17.84 mm, and that number is not noise to be rounded away: it is
the length of the absent vessel, measured on the meshes that survive it. Every other gap in this corpus is
prose. This one has a figure attached, and the figure tells an acquisition decision how much anatomy is
actually missing. Future derive tools should look for gaps that can be quantified this way rather than only
for contacts that can be located.

**Two independent tools agreeing is the only real check on a derived landmark.** Run 24 learned that a
measured number is not automatically a true one, after the aortic hiatus was nearly placed in the pelvis.
The defence found this run is cheaper than it sounds: write the second tool without consulting the first,
then compare the overlapping anchors. Four matched to three decimals and a fifth turned out to be the same
measurement expressed on a different parent. Had one genuinely disagreed, the disagreement would have been
the finding. This is worth making a habit wherever two scenes touch the same meshes — and the comparison
has to be run, not eyeballed, because the fifth pair looked like a match until it was.

**Distinguish "not in the catalog" from "not modellable".** The abdominal wall gaps split cleanly in two.
A brachiocephalic trunk or an oesophagus mesh is an acquisition away. A tendinous intersection and an
arcuate line are not: they are markings and edges, and no surface catalog of whole organs will ever carry
them. The first kind belongs on a shopping list; the second belongs in a note explaining that a different
kind of asset — a labelled overlay or an SVG diagram — is the only thing that will ever close it. Writing
both as flat "missing" lines would have kept a permanently open item on a list of buyable ones.

**Performance is a correctness issue in a derive tool.** The first nearest-point search ran for three
minutes without returning, because the shell walk rescanned the whole cube for every radius and the initial
bound was infinite. A tool that hangs does not get debugged under time pressure; it gets replaced by an
anchor typed in by eye, which is the exact failure this corpus is built to prevent. Seeding the search with
a coarse brute-force pass and precomputing the shell offsets brought both tools to under 1.3 s, and the
comment in `derive-great-vessel-landmarks.mjs` says why out loud so the next author does not undo it.

### Still waiting

**A brachiocephalic trunk mesh is now the highest-value single acquisition for the thorax**, and this run
can put a number on it: 17.84 mm of unmodelled artery that three scenes reference. **A pericardium mesh**
remains close behind. **SVG artwork remains the single biggest blocker overall**, ten `planned` scenes
authored and rendering nothing. **The four-chamber ingest** is still six ids and a script already written.
**Local ingest of the abdominal wall set** — `FMA13378`, `FMA11336`, `FMA7488`, `FMA8248`, `FMA22346`,
`FMA22347`, `FMA13892`, `FMA22344`, `FMA21964`, `FMA16587` — would let the next three scenes in this topic
be measured on both sides instead of the right only; all ten are in the catalog, none is in `meshes/`.
**Nerve and vein meshes** remain the highest-value acquisition overall, twenty-five runs old and unchanged.

## 2026-08-27T13:10Z — run 27 · Flat abdominal muscles · Inguinal canal

**Cursor-driven, not demand-driven.** `DEMAND.json` does not exist in the repo, so the two structures came
from the cursor as `sync-state.mjs` reported it: gross / Anterior Abdominal Wall & Inguinal Region /
Flat abdominal muscles, then Inguinal canal. `sync-state.mjs` ran first and reported STATE.json already in
step — 57 covered by 52 files, cursor at topic 11, structure 1.

**Both authored as `3d_anatomy`**, the first entry in each structure's `preferred_modes`, with `diagram`
recorded as `deferred_modes`.

**Models resolved.** Flat abdominal muscles — all six flat muscles of both sides: `FMA13336`/`FMA13337`
external oblique, `FMA13892`/`FMA13893` internal oblique, `FMA22344`/`FMA22345` transversus abdominis, plus
`FMA11336` linea alba as a part and both recti, both inguinal ligaments, both hip bones and the fifth and
twelfth ribs as context. Inguinal canal — `FMA13336`, `FMA13892`, `FMA22344`, `FMA21964` as the four walls,
`FMA7211` right testis as the destination, `FMA16586`/`FMA16587`, `FMA13377`, `FMA18806`, `FMA18885` as
context. Every id was looked up in `available-meshes.json` by the catalog's own name; none was copied from
another scene without checking.

**Models NOT found.** No aponeurosis of any kind (the catalog has whole muscles only) · no arcuate line ·
no conjoint tendon · no transversalis fascia · no Camper's or Scarpa's fascia · no thoracolumbar fascia ·
no lacunar ligament · no deep or superficial inguinal ring · no spermatic cord, vas deferens, pampiniform
plexus or cremaster · no inferior epigastric artery · no ilioinguinal, iliohypogastric or genitofemoral
nerve · no thoracoabdominal nerve. All recorded in the two `gaps[]` arrays, split between what an
acquisition could buy and what a surface catalog of organs will never carry.

**New tool: `tools/derive-flat-muscle-landmarks.mjs`.** Emitted nine landmarks in 1.2 s after re-proving the
LPS frame on the meshes it was handed. Seven on the right external oblique — rib five origin 0.55 mm, rib
twelve origin 0.65 mm, iliac crest insertion 0.22 mm, aponeurosis over the rectus 0.34 mm, and the lower,
lateral and upper extremes — plus two on the right hip bone, the symphyseal surface and the summit of the
crest. Eight went into the flat-muscle scene, three into the canal scene.

**Cross-check passed.** The external-oblique-to-rectus contact came out at 0.34 mm, reproducing
`derive-abdominal-wall-landmarks.mjs`'s measurement of the same physical contact from the rectus side. Two
tools, written independently against the same two meshes, agreeing on the gap. The anchors sit on different
parents by construction, so the `uvw` values differ and only the gap is comparable — that distinction is
stated in both `calibrated_by` notes rather than left for a reader to work out.

**Validator: PASS, both scenes, all eight stages.** 54/54 valid; `--mark` changed nothing. Flat abdominal
muscles: 23 structures (15 parts) · 7 views · 35 ops · ready. Inguinal canal: 13 structures (8 parts) ·
7 views · 38 ops · ready. Both degrade `PEEL_LAYER` only. **Index rebuilt:** 54 scenes, 39 ready, 4790 term
mappings, up from 4585. **`sync-state.mjs` re-run:** gross 44/81, cursor advanced to Rectus sheath.

**HELD — 15 structures, unchanged.** No new blocks, no new holds.

### What this run learned

**A space can be a first-class subject if you author its boundaries.** The inguinal canal is a hole, and the
tempting response was to defer it to `diagram` until an overlay exists. That would have been the wrong call
twice over: the four walls are all real meshes, and the walls ARE the exam answer. What the 3D scene cannot
do is mark the two rings, so the scene places them in words against two measured bony landmarks and says in
`gaps[]` that this is the biggest loss. Deferring would have hidden a topic that is three-quarters
renderable today behind artwork that does not exist.

**Naming the distance to the structure you cannot mark is better than marking it approximately.** The pubic
tubercle is the single most useful point in the groin and it is not a mesh. The anchor emitted is the
symphyseal surface of the pubis, and its label says the tubercle lies about two centimetres lateral. A
student reading "pubic tubercle" on a marker two centimetres off would learn the wrong point and never know.
A student reading "the tubercle is two centimetres lateral to this" learns both the point and the limit of
the model. The same pattern is used for the anterior superior iliac spine and for the superficial ring.

**The catalog has no aponeuroses, and that is the structural gap in the whole abdominal wall.** Three runs
have now hit it from different directions — the rectus sheath, the flat muscles, the canal walls. Each flat
muscle ships as one object from fleshy origin to tendinous end, so the single fact this region turns on —
that a muscle becomes a sheet and the sheet then chooses a side of the rectus — cannot be shown changing,
only described. This is not a missing organ that an acquisition would fix; it is a modelling decision in the
source data. It belongs on a different list from the missing nerves.

### Still waiting

**An inferior epigastric artery mesh is now the highest-value single acquisition for the abdomen** — it is
the landmark that separates indirect from direct hernia and the cause of a rectus sheath haematoma, and two
scenes now reference it in narration only. **A brachiocephalic trunk mesh** remains the equivalent for the
thorax, 17.84 mm of unmodelled artery. **SVG artwork remains the biggest blocker overall**, ten `planned`
scenes rendering nothing. **Local ingest of the abdominal wall set** is now more valuable than last run and
the list is shorter: `FMA13892` internal oblique, `FMA22344` transversus abdominis and `FMA21964` inguinal
ligament would let the arching roof of the canal, the conjoint tendon and the shutter be measured where they
actually are, instead of every anchor in three scenes hanging on the one muscle that happens to be on disk.
**Nerve and vein meshes** remain the highest-value acquisition overall, twenty-seven runs old and unchanged.

`FMA22450` pectineus, `FMA22456` adductor longus, `FMA13377` rectus abdominis, `FMA13892` internal oblique,
`FMA22344` transversus abdominis, `FMA11336` linea alba — 22 structures, 11 parts. Every id was looked up in

over from another scene on trust.

**Models NOT found.** No rectus sheath as an object · no arcuate line · no aponeurosis separable from its
muscle belly · no transversalis fascia, extraperitoneal fat or parietal peritoneum · no tendinous
intersections · no superior or inferior epigastric artery · no thoracoabdominal nerve · no pubic tubercle
as a distinguishable feature · no deep or superficial inguinal ring · no lacunar or pectineal ligament ·
no femoral sheath or femoral canal · no femoral, ilioinguinal or genitofemoral nerve · no spermatic cord,
round ligament or conjoint tendon. All recorded across the two `gaps[]` arrays and split, as before, between

**New tool: `tools/derive-inguinal-sheath-landmarks.mjs`.** Emitted eleven landmarks after re-proving the
LPS frame on the meshes it was handed. Six on the right hip bone — ASIS as a constrained anterior extreme,
summit of the iliac crest, pubic crest as a hip-bone-to-rectus contact at 0.29 mm, symphyseal surface as the
most medial vertex, and the two midpoints computed from those measured points — and five on the right rectus
abdominis: the anterior sheath contact at 0.34 mm, a constrained posterior extreme, the linea semilunaris,
and the upper and lower ends. Five went into the sheath scene, six into the landmarks scene.

**Cross-check passed, third time.** The external-oblique-to-rectus contact came out at 0.34 mm again,
reproducing both `derive-abdominal-wall-landmarks.mjs` and `derive-flat-muscle-landmarks.mjs` on the same

**Validator: PASS, both scenes, all eight stages.** 56/56 valid; `--mark` changed nothing. Rectus sheath:
20 structures (11 parts) · 6 views · 31 ops · ready. Inguinal ligament & landmarks: 22 structures (11 parts)
· 6 views · 33 ops · ready. Both degrade `PEEL_LAYER` only. **Index rebuilt:** 56 scenes, 41 ready, 5011 term
mappings, up from 4790. **`sync-state.mjs` re-run:** gross 46/81, cursor advanced to Stomach & Intestines /
Stomach — the first topic outside the body wall.

**HELD — 15 structures, unchanged.** No new blocks, no new holds.

### What this run learned

**An unconstrained extreme can be confidently, silently wrong.** The obvious way to anchor "the posterior
surface of rectus abdominis" is the most posterior vertex of the mesh. That point is at the PUBIC end, where
the muscle curves backwards to its origin — the exact coordinates the rectus scene already publishes as
`pubic_origin`. It is a real measurement of the wrong thing, and nothing about it looks wrong: it is
measured, reproducible, and lands on the muscle. It was caught only because two anchors derived for
different reasons came out with identical `uvw`. The fix was to constrain the search to the middle 40% of
the muscle's height and to record the constraint AND the reason for it in `calibrated_by`. The same pattern
saved the ASIS, where the unconstrained most-anterior point of the hip bone is the pubis. **Two anchors
sharing coordinates should be treated as a failed assertion, not a coincidence** — a cheap check worth
building into the derive tools.

**A midpoint of two measured points is a definition executed, not an estimate.** The mid-inguinal point is
*defined* as halfway between the ASIS and the pubic symphysis. Once both ends are measured, computing the
midpoint introduces no new error and no new judgement, and the result correctly lies off the bone surface
in soft tissue — which is exactly where the femoral artery is. This is a third derivation kind alongside
EXTREME and CONTACT, and it is the honest way to place surface-anatomy points that are not on any structure.

**Naming what was measured, not what was wanted, twice in the same region.** The pubic tubercle is the most
useful point in the groin and nothing in the geometry distinguishes it from the rest of the pubic crest. Run
26 handled this by anchoring the symphyseal surface and saying the tubercle is two centimetres lateral; this
run anchors the pubic crest — the measured rectus contact, which is closer — and says the tubercle is at its
lateral end. Both scenes teach the hernia rule against a point that is not quite the tubercle and both say
so in the label, the `calibrated_by` and the `gaps[]`. The alternative, a marker reading "pubic tubercle" a
centimetre off, teaches a wrong point that the student has no way to detect.

**Two spaces in one topic, both authored rather than deferred.** The inguinal canal (run 26) and the rectus
sheath (this run) are both holes, and both were tempting to route to `diagram` until artwork exists. Both
are three-quarters renderable today through the structures that bound them, and in both cases the boundaries
are the exam answer. Deferring either would have hidden a mostly-renderable topic behind artwork that does
not exist. The rule that made this decision twice — teach it through the adjacent structures that do exist —
is worth more than the meshes it substituted for.

### Still waiting

**An inferior epigastric artery mesh remains the highest-value single acquisition for the abdomen** — three
scenes now reference it in narration only, and it is simultaneously the cause of the rectus sheath
haematoma, the landmark separating direct from indirect hernia, and half of the anastomosis inside the
sheath. **A brachiocephalic trunk mesh** remains the thoracic equivalent. **SVG artwork remains the biggest
blocker overall**, ten `planned` scenes rendering nothing. **Local ingest of the abdominal wall set** is
still the cheapest unlock and the list is unchanged: `FMA13892` internal oblique, `FMA22344` transversus
abdominis and `FMA21964` inguinal ligament. Four scenes in this topic now hang every anchor on the two right
sided meshes that happen to be on disk, and the inguinal ligament's own course is authored as a trace
between bony points because the ligament could not be measured at all. **Nerve and vein meshes** remain the
highest-value acquisition overall, twenty-eight runs old and unchanged.

## Run 28 — 2026-08-27

**Cursor-driven, not demand-driven.** `DEMAND.json` does not exist, so there was no evidence file to
override curriculum order. `sync-state.mjs` was run first, as always: it reported 46/81 gross, 61 structures
covered by 56 files, STATE.json already in step, and named **gross / Stomach & Intestines / Stomach** as

`3d_anatomy`, so both were authored as full v2 3D scenes with `diagram` recorded as `deferred_modes`.

**Models resolved — Stomach (17 structures, 9 parts).** `FMA7148` stomach · `FMA7131` esophagus ·
`FMA7206` duodenum · `FMA50737` celiac artery · `FMA14768` left gastric artery · `FMA14773` splenic artery ·
`FMA14771` common hepatic artery · `FMA14331` splenic vein · `FMA14332` superior mesenteric vein · context

`FMA7205` left kidney, `FMA15630` left adrenal gland, `FMA3784` descending aorta.

**Models resolved — Small intestine (16 structures, 8 parts).** `FMA7206` duodenum · `FMA7207` jejunum ·
`FMA7208` ileum · `FMA14749` superior mesenteric artery · `FMA14332` superior mesenteric vein ·
`FMA10419` pancreatic duct · `FMA7202` gallbladder · `FMA14542` appendix · context `FMA7148` stomach,
`FMA7197` liver, `FMA7204` right kidney, `FMA7205` left kidney, `FMA3784` descending aorta,
`FMA50737` celiac artery, `FMA14750` inferior mesenteric artery, `FMA14544` rectum.

**Models NOT found — searched the catalog by name, nothing matched.** No fundus, body, pyloric antrum,
pyloric canal, cardia, pylorus, greater curvature, lesser curvature or incisura angularis. No **pancreas**
(only `FMA10419`, its duct). No **caecum, colon, ileocaecal valve or any large bowel** — 934 models and the
only large-gut meshes are the appendix and the rectum. No peritoneum, mesentery, omentum or lesser sac. No
gastroduodenal, right gastric, gastro-omental, short gastric, jejunal or ileal artery; no pancreaticoduodenal
arcade. No portal vein. No vagus nerve or vagal trunks — no abdominal nerve of any kind is in the catalog.
No duodenal subdivisions, major or minor papilla, duodenojejunal flexure or ligament of Treitz.

**Validator: PASS, both scenes, all eight stages.** 58/58 valid; `--mark` changed nothing and no scene was
left blocked. Stomach: 17 structures (9 parts) · 7 views · 44 ops · ready. Small intestine: 16 structures
(8 parts) · 7 views · 42 ops · ready. Both degrade `PEEL_LAYER` only. **Index rebuilt:** 58 scenes, 43
ready, 5225 term mappings, up from 5011. **`sync-state.mjs` re-run:** gross 48/81, 63 structures covered by
58 files, cursor advanced to **Large intestine** in the same topic.

**HELD — 15 structures, unchanged.** No new blocks, no new holds. One typo was caught before the gate: a
structure key was written `imá` with an accent. The ops stage would have resolved it, because the ops
referenced the same broken string — a key and its references are consistent even when both are wrong, and
nothing downstream compares a key against anything human. Fixed by hand.

### What this run learned

**Two structures in the same topic, one that will not divide and one that divides perfectly.** The stomach
is a single mesh whose five regions are the whole of the first-year answer. The small intestine is three
meshes named exactly as the curriculum names them. The same authoring rule produced completely different
scenes: for the stomach, build outwards into inlet, outlet, arteries and bed until the parts list carries
real teaching; for the small intestine, build inwards and let the three parts do the work. **The rule is
not "find the parts" — it is "find something with a mesh that the exam actually asks about."**

**A bed is a better substitute for a subdivision than a guessed marker.** Neither scene has a single anchor,
because none of these meshes is on disk and this corpus does not place anchors by eye. What replaced them
was posterior relations: the stomach bed as a group that beat 3 isolates from behind, and the duodenum's
four parts traced by lighting the pancreatic duct, right kidney, aorta and left kidney in turn. A relation
is a measured object in its own right. It teaches the subdivision by teaching what is behind it, which is
how both are examined anyway, and it makes no claim about a location the geometry cannot support.

**The appendix as the only handle on the large bowel is worth stating plainly.** It is not a substitute for
the caecum; it is a landmark attached to it. The label says `Appendix — the end of the road` in the
student's own parts list, not only in `gaps[]`, because a student who sees the ileum end at something small
and worm-like should not conclude that is what it ends at. **When rule 2(b) is used, the label is where the
honesty has to live** — `gaps[]` is read by maintainers, and the label is read by the person being taught.

**A context structure that supplies nothing needs a reason in its own narration.** The inferior mesenteric
artery is in the small intestine scene solely to fix the T12/L1/L3 sequence. Left unexplained, its presence
in a small-bowel scene is itself a claim. Its narration says it supplies no part of the small intestine.

### Still waiting

**A pancreas mesh is now the highest-value single acquisition for the abdomen.** Two scenes in this run
represent it with its own duct and both say so in a label. It is the bed of the stomach, the curve of the
duodenum, the source of the ampulla, and the answer to a posterior penetrating ulcer.
**A caecum and colon set is the second** — three of the five structures left in this topic (Large intestine,
Peritoneum & mesentery, Gut blood supply) depend on bowel the catalog does not hold at all, and the next
cursor position is Large intestine, which has no mesh whatsoever. **That structure should be assessed for
its deferred `diagram` mode on the next run rather than forced into 3D.**
**A gastroduodenal artery mesh** remains the cheapest fix for the ulcer teaching in the stomach scene.
**Local ingest of the abdominal viscera set** (`FMA7148`, `FMA7206`, `FMA7207`, `FMA7208`) is the cheapest
way to get anchors into this topic at all; today it has none. **Peritoneum and mesentery remain
unrepresentable** and now block half of a spotter answer, not just a footnote. **Nerve and vein meshes**
remain the highest-value acquisition overall, twenty-nine runs old and unchanged.

## Run 29 — 2026-08-27 · Large intestine, Peritoneum & mesentery

**Cursor-driven, not demand-driven.** `viz-training/DEMAND.json` does not exist, so there was no evidence
list to outrank the curriculum. `sync-state.mjs` ran first and reported STATE.json already in step — 63
structures covered by 58 files, cursor at **gross / Stomach & Intestines / Large intestine**. Took that and
the next uncovered structure in the same topic, **Peritoneum & mesentery**.

**Structures attempted: 2. Scenes written: 2. Neither is `ready`, and neither failed the gate.**

### Large intestine → `3d_anatomy`, status `candidate`

**Models resolved (6 parts, 12 context).** `FMA76893` free taenia · `FMA76891` mesocolic taenia ·
`FMA76892` omental taenia · `FMA14542` appendix · `FMA14544` rectum · `FMA21930` external anal sphincter.
Context: `FMA7208` ileum, `FMA14749` SMA, `FMA14750` IMA, `FMA14332` SMV, `FMA7197` liver, `FMA7196` spleen,
`FMA7148` stomach, `FMA7204` / `FMA7205` kidneys, `FMA3784` aorta, `FMA16202` sacrum, `FMA15900` bladder.

**Models NOT found.** No caecum. No colon of any kind — ascending, transverse, descending or sigmoid. No
colic flexure. No haustrum, sacculation or appendix epiploica. No ileocaecal valve. No anal canal, internal
anal sphincter or pectinate line. No ileocolic, right colic, middle colic, left colic, sigmoid or superior
rectal artery, and **no marginal artery of Drummond**. No mesocolon, no peritoneum.

**The catalog search that was wrong last run.** Run 28 recorded "934 models and the only large-gut meshes
are the appendix and the rectum." That is false: all three taeniae coli are in the catalog. The run-28
search covered *colon, caecum, cecum, bowel, intestine* and never the word *taenia*, so the meshes that
carry the entire course of the colon were reported as absent by a search that could not have found them.
**A negative catalog result is a statement about the query, not about the catalog.** The correction is
written into the new scene's `gaps[]`; the stale note in `gross__stomach-intestines__small-intestine` is
flagged there for a human to fix, because a scene does not edit another scene's file.

**Why `candidate`.** All eight stages pass. The scene teaches the taeniae, the two ends, the pectinate line,
the watershed and the four-feature spotter contrast — but the tube itself has no mesh, so it cannot answer
*identify the structure arrowed*, and a scene that cannot do that is not shippable to a student. Ingesting
caecum and colon meshes promotes it with no re-authoring: the beats, ops and narration already accept them.

### Peritoneum & mesentery → `diagram`, status `planned`

**Models resolved: none, and none was sought.** Searched all 934 entries for peritoneum, mesentery,
mesocolon, omentum, falciform, serous, bursa and sac — nothing. Routed to the second entry in its
`preferred_modes` with `provider.primary` `svg`, 22 authored regions and no refs, `deferred_modes:
["3d_anatomy"]`. `preferred_modes` in the curriculum was not touched.

**Third serous membrane, same failure.** Pleura, pericardium, peritoneum. The free set is solid organs,
muscles, bones and vessels. **This has stopped being a coverage gap and is now a provider limit** — no
further ingest of the same archive closes it, and three separate curriculum entries are waiting on it.

**Validator: PASS, both scenes, all eight stages.** `--mark` changed nothing; no scene was left blocked.
60/60 valid. Large intestine: 18 structures (6 parts) · 7 views · 40 ops · `candidate` · degrades
`PEEL_LAYER`. Peritoneum: 22 structures (18 parts) · 7 views · 34 ops · `planned` · every op warned as
unknown to the `svg` adapter, which is expected and non-fatal, exactly as for the pleura scene.
**Index rebuilt:** 60 scenes, 43 ready, **5485 term mappings**, up from 5225.
**`sync-state.mjs` re-run:** 65 structures attended by 60 files, gross 48/81 ready, cursor advanced to
**Gut blood supply (coeliac/SMA/IMA)** — the last structure in this topic.

**HELD — 17 structures, up from 15.** Both of this run's scenes join the list. No blocks.

### What this run learned

**The strongest use of rule 2(b) so far, and it came from a mesh nobody had looked for.** The three taeniae
are not a substitute for the colon in the way the appendix is a substitute for the caecum. They are *on*
the colon along its whole length, so tracing them traces the organ. The scene shows the course, both
flexures by their relations, the convergence at the appendix base and the fanning-out at the rectosigmoid
junction — most of a large intestine viva — from three thin bands. **Substituting an adjacent structure
works best when the substitute is coextensive with the thing that is missing.**

**Two structures, two different reasons for not being `ready`, and the distinction matters.** The
peritoneum is `planned` because no engine input exists — artwork has to be drawn. The large intestine is
`candidate` because the scene is renderable today and still not good enough to teach from. Both are
`covers`-declared, so `sync-state.mjs` lists them as HELD rather than leaving them on the worklist to be
re-authored, and both stay visible every run until someone decides. **Held is the honest state for work
that is finished but not shippable; the alternative is either lying in `covers[]` or authoring it again.**

**A stale gap note is more dangerous than a missing one.** Run 28's claim about large-bowel meshes would
have been read by run 29 as settled fact, and the obvious next move — route Large intestine to `diagram`
because it has no mesh at all, as run 28 explicitly recommended — would have thrown away six real models.
The instruction to resolve every part **by looking it up in the catalog** is what caught it: the lookup was
done from the catalog, not from the previous run's prose. **Never inherit a negative finding.**

### Still waiting

**A caecum and colon set is now the highest-value single acquisition for the abdomen**, ahead of the
pancreas. It is the difference between one `candidate` and one `ready` scene, it would complete the last
structure in this topic, and it is the only thing standing between the corpus and a finished gut.
**A pancreas mesh** remains second; three scenes now represent it by its duct alone.
**Peritoneal and serous membrane models are a provider question, not an ingest question** — pleura,
pericardium and peritoneum are all authored, all `planned`, and all waiting on the same decision.
**The marginal artery of Drummond** is the cheapest single vessel that would improve the colon teaching.
**Local ingest of the abdominal set** (`FMA14542`, `FMA14544`, `FMA76893`) would give this topic its first
anchors — McBurney's point, the rectosigmoid junction and the anorectal ring are all measurable contacts.
**Nerve and vein meshes** remain the highest-value acquisition overall, thirty runs old and unchanged.

## Run 30 — 2026-08-27 · Gut blood supply (coeliac/SMA/IMA), Liver

**Cursor-driven, not demand-driven.** `viz-training/DEMAND.json` does not exist, so there was no evidence
file to outrank the curriculum and the run took the next two structures from the cursor:
`gross/Stomach & Intestines/Gut blood supply (coeliac/SMA/IMA)` (topic 12, structure 4) and then the first
structure of topic 13, `gross/Liver, Biliary Tract, Pancreas & Spleen/Liver`.

**`sync-state.mjs` ran first and reported STATE.json already in step** — 65 structures covered by 60 files,
gross 48/81, 17 HELD. No drift to correct this run, but the run was still chosen from the printed coverage
table rather than from anything remembered.

### Gut blood supply (coeliac/SMA/IMA) → `diagram`, status `planned`

**Mode was decided by the curriculum's ordering, and this is the first run where that mattered in the
direction nobody expects.** `preferred_modes` is `["diagram","3d_anatomy"]`. The SVG engine renders
`diagram` today, so `diagram` is the first renderable mode and the scene was authored as a drawing —
**even though the models exist**. `FMA50737` coeliac, `FMA14749` superior mesenteric, `FMA14750` inferior
mesenteric, plus `FMA14768`, `FMA14773`, `FMA14771`, `FMA3784`, `FMA14331`, `FMA14332`, `FMA7206`,
`FMA7207`, `FMA7208`, `FMA14542`, `FMA14544` were all found in the catalog and all deliberately not used.

Every previous deferral in this corpus was forced by absence. This one is a positive curriculum judgement:
the teaching point is *which artery owns which stretch of gut*, and the two junction lines — the major
duodenal papilla and the two-thirds point on the transverse colon — are the answer. Three arteries rendered
in front of an aorta would show none of it. `deferred_modes: ["3d_anatomy"]` records the upgrade, and the
resolvable ids are written into `gaps[]` so the promotion costs nobody a second catalog sweep.

**Models NOT found:** no colon of any kind (caecum, ascending, transverse, descending, sigmoid, flexures);
no portal vein; no inferior mesenteric vein; no gastroduodenal artery; no colic arteries; no rectal
arteries; no marginal artery; no splanchnic nerves. Twenty-nine authored regions, no refs, `provider.primary`
`svg`. `preferred_modes` in CURRICULUM.json was not touched.

**Does not claim `Portal venous system`.** Beat 6 teaches the confluence and the porto-systemic escape
routes because gut venous drainage cannot be taught without them, but that entry stays on the worklist.

### Liver → `3d_anatomy`, status `ready`

**Models resolved (13), all looked up by catalog `name`:** `FMA7197` liver · `FMA7202` gallbladder ·
`FMA10951` inferior vena cava · `FMA14771` common hepatic artery · `FMA50737` celiac artery ·
`FMA14331` splenic vein · `FMA14332` superior mesenteric vein · `FMA13295` diaphragm · `FMA7148` stomach ·
`FMA7131` esophagus · `FMA7206` duodenum · `FMA7204` right kidney · `FMA15629` right adrenal gland ·
`FMA10419` pancreatic duct · `FMA3784` descending aorta · `FMA8364` right ninth rib · `FMA8445` right tenth
rib. Names copied verbatim, including the catalog's American spellings `celiac artery`, `esophagus` and
`right adrenal gland`, which the labels then render as the British forms the student reads.

**Rule 2(b) applied to a plane, not an organ.** Cantlie's line has no mesh and never will — it is a plane,
not a thing. But it is *defined* by two structures that do exist: the gallbladder fossa in front and the
groove for the inferior vena cava behind. Beat 2 shows both and lets the plane fall between them, which
teaches the functional division, the quadrate lobe's true allegiance to the left half, and the caudate
lobe's independence — all from two meshes and a relationship.

**Models NOT found:** portal vein (taught as its two tributaries, with the narration saying plainly that
the portal vein does not exist until the confluence); bile duct, common hepatic duct, hepatic ducts, cystic
duct (so the third member of the triad is narrated, not drawn, and Calot's triangle cannot be shown at
all); hepatic veins; any lobe or Couinaud segment; falciform, coronary and triangular ligaments; lesser
omentum; pancreas.

**No anchors authored.** `FMA7197` is not in the local decimated set, so the porta hepatis, bare area,
gallbladder fossa, caval groove and the H of fissures could not be measured. None was typed in by eye.

**Does not claim `Biliary tree & gallbladder`.** The gallbladder appears as the marker of the interlobar
plane and as a boundary of Calot's triangle, not as a taught organ with ducts and stones. That entry is the
next structure at the cursor and it is blocked on the missing bile ducts.

### Tooling

**Validator: PASS, both scenes, all eight stages. 62/62 valid.** `--mark` changed nothing; no scene was
left blocked, and none of the 60 existing scenes regressed.
Liver: 17 structures (7 parts) · 7 views · 53 ops · `ready` · degrades `PEEL_LAYER`.
Gut blood supply: 29 structures (28 parts) · 7 views · 41 ops · `planned` · every op warned as unknown to
the `svg` adapter, which is expected and non-fatal, as for the pleura and peritoneum scenes.
**Index rebuilt:** 62 scenes, 44 ready, **5791 term mappings**, up from 5485.
**`sync-state.mjs` re-run:** gross 49/81 ready, 67/81 attended, Stomach & Intestines now 5 of 5 attended,
cursor advanced to **Liver, Biliary Tract, Pancreas & Spleen / Biliary tree & gallbladder**.
**HELD — 18 structures, up from 17.** Gut blood supply joins the list. No blocks.

### What this run learned

**`preferred_modes` is a rule, not a hint, and it cuts both ways.** Twenty-nine runs of this corpus have
read the ordered list as a way to survive missing models. This run is the first time following it meant
declining models that were sitting right there. The instruction says *take the first mode an engine can
render today* — it does not say *take 3D whenever 3D is possible*. A corpus that quietly upgrades whenever
the catalog allows is a corpus whose curriculum intent is decided by provider coverage, which is exactly
the failure the spec was written to prevent. **Provider coverage must not decide pedagogy in either
direction.**

**A substitution is only honest if the student can see it.** The liver scene teaches the portal vein
through the splenic and superior mesenteric veins, and the narration says outright that there is no portal
vein until they meet. Compare the failure mode the validator exists to catch: `FMA7196` labelled "left
lung". The difference between a legitimate rule-2(b) substitution and a lie is not which mesh is on screen
— it is whether the narration names the substitution. **Say what is standing in for what.**

**Two scenes, two different absences, and only one of them is a gap.** The gut blood supply scene lists
missing meshes in `gaps[]` but is not *waiting* on them — it is waiting on artwork, because it was never
going to be 3D this run. The liver scene's gaps are real blockers on teaching that would otherwise be
clickable. Writing both under the same heading risks a future run reading the first list as a reason to
promote a scene that was deferred on purpose, so the gut blood supply `gaps[]` opens by saying why the mode
was chosen before it says anything about what is missing.

### Still waiting

**Bile ducts are now the blocking acquisition** — `Biliary tree & gallbladder` is the next structure at the
cursor and there is no duct of any kind in 934 entries. Without them that scene is a gallbladder floating
next to a liver.
**A caecum and colon set** remains the highest-value acquisition for the abdomen, unchanged from run 29,
and it now blocks a second scene: the gut blood supply scene's three colonic beats and both watersheds.
**A portal vein mesh** has moved up sharply: it is now named as the top gap in two consecutive scenes and
is the whole of the still-unauthored `Portal venous system`.
**A pancreas mesh** — four scenes now represent the gland by its duct alone.
**Peritoneal and serous membrane models remain a provider question, not an ingest question.** Pleura,
pericardium, peritoneum, and now the liver's falciform and coronary ligaments.
**Local ingest of `FMA7197`** is the cheapest single anchor unlock in the abdomen: six landmarks on one
mesh, and it would make three of the liver scene's seven beats clickable rather than narrated.
**Nerve and vein meshes** remain the highest-value acquisition overall, thirty-one runs old and unchanged.
