## Run 31 — 2026-08-27T18:0xZ · Biliary tree & gallbladder, Pancreas

**Cursor-driven, not demand-driven.** `viz-training/DEMAND.json` does not exist, so there was no evidence
file to outrank the curriculum. The run took the next two structures from the cursor:
`gross/Liver, Biliary Tract, Pancreas & Spleen/Biliary tree & gallbladder` (topic 13, structure 1) and
`.../Pancreas` (structure 2).

**`sync-state.mjs` ran first, before anything was read or decided,** and reported STATE.json already in
step: 67 structures covered by 62 files, gross 49/81 ready, 18 HELD. The run was chosen from that printed
table, not from anything remembered.

### Biliary tree & gallbladder → `3d_anatomy`, status `candidate`

`preferred_modes` is `["3d_anatomy","diagram"]`; 3D is renderable, so `diagram` went to `deferred_modes`.

**Models resolved (12), every one looked up by the catalog's own `name`:** `FMA7202` gallbladder ·
`FMA7197` liver · `FMA10419` pancreatic duct · `FMA7206` duodenum · `FMA14771` common hepatic artery ·
`FMA50737` celiac artery · `FMA14332` superior mesenteric vein · `FMA14331` splenic vein · `FMA10951`
inferior vena cava · `FMA7148` stomach · `FMA8364` right ninth rib · `FMA13295` diaphragm. Names copied
verbatim including the catalog's `celiac artery`; the label renders the British spelling the student reads.

**Models NOT found — and this is the whole reason the scene is not `ready`.** A sweep of all 934 entries
for bile, biliar, gall, cystic, hepatic duct, choledoch, ampulla, papilla, vater and sphincter returns
exactly two hits: `gallbladder` and `pancreatic duct`. So the right and left hepatic ducts, the common
hepatic duct, the cystic duct and the common bile duct — the entire tree named in the structure's own title
— have no geometry at all. Also absent: cystic artery (taught through its parent, the common hepatic
artery, rule 2(a)); sphincter of Oddi and major duodenal papilla (the duodenum is one undivided model);
lesser omentum and hepatoduodenal ligament (the standing peritoneal absence).

**Why `candidate` and not `blocked`, and why it still `covers` the structure.** The validator passes it —
nothing is invented and every name matches — so `blocked` would be false. But half the subject is
narration, so `ready` would also be false. `candidate` is the honest status, and the coverage rule works
as designed: because the scene is not ready, `sync-state.mjs` now lists this structure as HELD rather than
done, so it stays visible for a decision instead of disappearing off the worklist.

**Rule 2(b) applied twice, and named in the labels both times.** The pancreatic duct carries the label
"the bile duct's partner at the ampulla" because it genuinely ends where the bile duct ends — it marks the
destination correctly while being the wrong half of the common channel, and the narration says so. The
common hepatic artery carries "parent of the cystic artery" for the same reason. The difference between a
legitimate substitution and a lie is whether the narration names it.

**Bile is `concept:bile-storage` and `concept:bile-release`, plus `concept:gallstone` in beat 4.** These
are the declared abstraction for a path that is anatomy while its subject is not a model — the same device
the liver scene used for portal blood. Each path walks only real meshes.

**No anchors authored.** `FMA7202` is not in the local decimated set, so the fundus, Hartmann's pouch, the
neck and the gallbladder fossa could not be measured on geometry. None was typed in by eye.

**Does not claim `Pancreas` or `Portal venous system`** even though the pancreatic duct and both portal
tributaries appear.

### Pancreas → `3d_anatomy`, status `candidate`

`preferred_modes` is `["3d_anatomy","diagram"]`; `diagram` deferred.

**Models resolved (16):** `FMA10419` pancreatic duct · `FMA7206` duodenum · `FMA14773` splenic artery ·
`FMA14331` splenic vein · `FMA14332` superior mesenteric vein · `FMA14749` superior mesenteric artery ·
`FMA50737` celiac artery · `FMA7196` spleen · `FMA7148` stomach · `FMA7202` gallbladder · `FMA7197` liver ·
`FMA7205` left kidney · `FMA15630` left adrenal gland · `FMA3784` descending aorta · `FMA10951` inferior
vena cava · `FMA13072` first lumbar vertebra.

**Models NOT found:** the pancreas itself. A sweep for pancreas, pancreatic, islet, acinus, uncinate, head,
neck, body and tail returns the pancreatic duct and nothing else. Also absent: portal vein; bile duct;
gastroduodenal, superior and inferior pancreaticoduodenal arteries and their arcades; lesser sac;
transverse mesocolon; splenorenal ligament.

**The judgement call this run, stated plainly.** The heart-chambers precedent says a structure whose
subject has no mesh at all gets routed to its deferred `diagram` mode. The large-intestine precedent says a
structure taught through a genuine sub-part that does exist stays `3d_anatomy` as a `candidate`. The
pancreas falls on the large-intestine side: `FMA10419` is not an adjacent organ standing in for the gland,
it is *inside* the gland and runs its full length, so it occupies the pancreas's exact position and axis
and the relations authored around it are all true. Every label and narration says the duct is standing for
the gland. Had there been no duct either, this would have been a `diagram`.

**Beat 3 is the one that most needed the missing mesh and least suffered from it.** Exocrine and endocrine
are separated by *destination* rather than by appearance — `concept:pancreatic-juice` to the duodenum,
`concept:islet-hormones` into the splenic and superior mesenteric veins to the liver — and both paths are
geometrically true at every waypoint. The islets themselves were never going to be renderable at this
scale; they belong to a histology scene, and the run did not pretend otherwise.

**No anchors authored,** same reason: neither `FMA10419` nor `FMA7206` is in the local decimated set.

**Does not claim `Spleen` or `Portal venous system`.**

### Gate and index

`validate-scenes.mjs --mark`: **64/64 valid, 0 blocked.** Both new scenes pass all eight stages; the only
capability note is `PEEL_LAYER` degrading on the bodyparts3d adapter, which is expected and recorded.
`build-scene-index.mjs`: **64 scenes (44 ready) · 6011 term mappings**, up from 5791.
`sync-state.mjs` re-run: **69 covered by 64 files**, gross 49/81 ready, **20 HELD** (the two new candidates
join the list, which is the correct outcome — they hold their slots without being counted as done).
Next at the cursor: `gross/Liver, Biliary Tract, Pancreas & Spleen/Spleen`.

### Still waiting

**Bile ducts have gone from "the next blocker" to a realised one.** Run 30 predicted the biliary scene
would be a gallbladder floating next to a liver; it is that, plus a correctly ordered narration and three
concept traces. A single `common bile duct` mesh would move this scene from `candidate` toward `ready` and
would also complete the portal triad in the liver scene. **Highest-value single acquisition in the abdomen.**
**A pancreas mesh** — now named by five scenes, and the reason `Pancreas` is a candidate rather than ready.
**A portal vein mesh** — named as a top gap in four consecutive scenes and the whole of the unauthored
`Portal venous system`, which is two structures away.
**A caecum and colon set** remains outstanding from run 29.
**Peritoneal and serous membranes remain a provider question, not an ingest question.**
**Local ingest of `FMA7197`, `FMA7202`, `FMA10419` and `FMA7206`** would unlock roughly a dozen measured
anchors across the four scenes of this topic and is the cheapest way to make narrated beats clickable.
**Nerve and vein meshes** remain the highest-value acquisition overall, thirty-two runs old and unchanged.

## Run 33 — 2026-08-27 · Kidney, Suprarenal (adrenal) gland

**Driven by the cursor, not by demand.** `DEMAND.json` does not exist in `viz-training/`, so the priority
rule did not apply and the run took the next two structures at the cursor:
`gross / Kidney & Posterior Abdominal Wall / Kidney` and `.../Suprarenal (adrenal) gland`.
`sync-state.mjs` ran first, as required, and reported STATE.json already in step (71 covered by 66 files).

### Discrepancy found before any authoring

**The previous run is undocumented.** `scenes/gross__liver-biliary-tract-pancreas-spleen__spleen.json` and
`scenes/gross__liver-biliary-tract-pancreas-spleen__portal-venous-system.json` both exist on disk and both
validate, but neither `CORPUS.md` nor `RUNLOG.md` contains a run 32 block — both files end at run 31
(biliary tree, pancreas) and CORPUS's summary line still reads "64 scenes". The scenes directory is the
source of truth and `sync-state.mjs` had already counted them, so nothing was re-authored and nothing is
broken; but the prose record of that run is missing and only a human can reconstruct why. Recorded here
rather than silently corrected. This run's own CORPUS totals are recomputed from disk, not carried forward.

### `gross__kidney-posterior-abdominal-wall__kidney`

`preferred_modes: ["3d_anatomy", "diagram"]` → authored `3d_anatomy`, `deferred_modes: ["diagram"]`.
27 structures, 13 parts, 6 views, 45 ops. Status **ready**.

**Models resolved (13 parts + 14 context, all checked against `available-meshes.json` by catalog name):**
`FMA7204` right kidney · `FMA7205` left kidney · `FMA14752` right renal artery · `FMA14753` left renal
artery · `FMA14335` right renal vein · `FMA14336` left renal vein · `FMA15571` right ureter · `FMA15572`
left ureter · `FMA22342`/`FMA22343` psoas major · `FMA22348`/`FMA22349` quadratus lumborum · `FMA22344`
transversus abdominis · `FMA13295` diaphragm · `FMA8531` eleventh rib · `FMA8533` twelfth rib · `FMA13072`
first lumbar vertebra · `FMA3784` descending aorta · `FMA10951` inferior vena cava · `FMA14749` superior
mesenteric artery · `FMA15629`/`FMA15630` suprarenal glands · `FMA7197` liver · `FMA7196` spleen · `FMA7206`
duodenum · `FMA10419` pancreatic duct · `FMA15900` urinary bladder.

**This is the best-resourced region the task has met since the vertebral column.** Both organs, both
arteries, both veins, both ureters and the entire muscular bed exist. That changed what could be taught:
beat 2 is authored from `posterior` and shows the four-part bed (psoas → quadratus lumborum → transversus
aponeurosis → diaphragm) as real muscle, with the twelfth rib highlighted and the eleventh-versus-twelfth
rule drawn rather than asserted. The clinically load-bearing sentence of the whole scene — every posterior
approach stays below the twelfth rib because the pleural recess dips behind its medial half — is made
against the bone itself.

**Models NOT found, and what was done instead.** No renal cortex, medulla, pyramid, papilla, column of
Bertin, minor or major calyx, or renal pelvis; no renal capsule, perirenal fat, renal fascia or pararenal
fat; no segmental arteries; no gonadal veins. The internal architecture and the four coverings — which are
the two things most reliably asked about the kidney — are therefore narrated over a `CROSS_SECTION` of the
solid organ, with `PEEL_LAYER` authored so the beat is already written when layered models exist. The
pelviureteric junction is shown as the point where the ureter mesh begins. All of it is in `gaps[]` and
every internal name is in `match.terms`.

**Two honest substitutions, both labelled.** The catalog's ribs are single-sided, so the eleventh rib in the
scene is `FMA8531`, the *right* eleventh, used to teach a relation that belongs to the *left* kidney; the
label and narration both say the eleventh crosses the left kidney only, so nothing is misrepresented, but a
student who rotates the scene sees it on the wrong side. And `FMA10419` stands for the pancreas under the
label "Pancreatic duct — marking the pancreas", the same convention run 31 established.

**No anchors authored.** Neither `FMA7204` nor `FMA7205` nor `FMA15571` is in the local decimated set, so
the hilum, the poles, the renal angle and the three ureteric constrictions could not be placed by
measurement. They were not typed in by eye.

**Does not claim `Ureters`, `Suprarenal (adrenal) gland` or `Abdominal aorta & IVC`,** all of which appear
in the scene and all of which remain separate curriculum entries on the worklist.

### `gross__kidney-posterior-abdominal-wall__suprarenal-adrenal-gland`

`preferred_modes: ["3d_anatomy", "diagram"]` → authored `3d_anatomy`, `deferred_modes: ["diagram"]`.
15 structures, 9 parts, 5 views, 37 ops. Status **ready**.

**Models resolved:** `FMA15629` right adrenal gland · `FMA15630` left adrenal gland · `FMA7204`/`FMA7205`
kidneys · `FMA10951` inferior vena cava · `FMA14336` left renal vein · `FMA3784` descending aorta ·
`FMA14752` right renal artery · `FMA13295` diaphragm · `FMA50737` celiac artery · `FMA7197` liver ·
`FMA7148` stomach · `FMA10419` pancreatic duct · `FMA7196` spleen · `FMA13072` first lumbar vertebra.

**Models NOT found:** right and left suprarenal veins; superior, middle and inferior suprarenal arteries;
inferior phrenic artery; adrenal cortex; adrenal medulla; zona glomerulosa, fasciculata and reticularis;
greater splanchnic nerve; coeliac plexus; peritoneum and lesser sac; pancreas.

**The judgement call this run.** The gland's two most examinable facts — three arteries in and one vein out,
and cortex versus medulla — have no meshes at all. On the heart-chambers precedent that would argue for
routing the structure to its deferred `diagram` mode. It was kept at `3d_anatomy` because both glands
themselves exist and are correctly shaped and positioned, and because every vessel in the teaching arises
from or drains into a vessel that *does* exist: the inferior phrenic from the diaphragm, the middle
suprarenal from the aorta, the inferior suprarenal from the renal artery, the right vein into the cava and
the left vein into the left renal vein. That is option (b) applied four times over, and beat 3 draws each
relation between two real structures. The internal zones are the weaker half, narrated over a cut of the
solid gland, and `gaps[]` says outright that a `diagram`-mode companion would teach them better than the 3D
model can — which is precisely what `deferred_modes` is for.

**The asymmetry is the teaching spine here too,** and it is the same asymmetry as the kidney scene: pyramidal
right gland wedged between cava and the bare area of the liver with a vein a few millimetres long, versus a
crescentic left gland reached across the lesser sac with a long vein into the left renal vein. Beat 3
carries the surgical consequence (the right vein avulses into a cava that cannot be clamped) and the
diagnostic one (adrenal venous sampling is easy left, hard right).

**No anchors authored** — same reason, neither gland mesh is local.

**Does not claim `Kidney` or `Abdominal aorta & IVC`.**

### Gate and index

`validate-scenes.mjs --mark`: **68/69 valid, 0 scenes newly blocked.** Both new scenes pass all eight
stages — schema, canonical, provider-id, existence, ops, capability, purity, lifecycle — with the only
notes being `PEEL_LAYER` degrading on the primary adapter, which is expected and recorded per scene.
The single failure is `scenes/__tmp-regression.json`, a stray copy of `tools/fixtures/regression-spleen.json`
left in the scenes directory by an earlier run. It produced exactly the required **11 errors across five
stages**, so the gate is intact. `--mark` rewrote that copy as `status:"blocked"`, which would have silently
degraded the fixture's own expectation on the next run, so it was restored byte-for-byte from
`tools/fixtures/regression-spleen.json` immediately afterwards. **It should be deleted from `scenes/` by a
human** — it is not a scene, it inflates `index.json` to 69, and `sync-state.mjs` flags it every run as
matching no curriculum structure.

`build-scene-index.mjs`: **69 entries (48 ready) · 6505 term mappings**, up from 6011 — those counts include
the stray fixture; the real corpus is 68 scenes, 47 ready.
`sync-state.mjs` re-run: **73 covered by 68 files**, gross 52/81 attended, **21 HELD** (unchanged — this run
added no candidates). Next at the cursor: `gross / Kidney & Posterior Abdominal Wall / Ureters`.

### Still waiting

**Renal internal structure is the new top gap in the abdomen.** Cortex, medulla, a pyramid, a calyx and a
renal pelvis would convert the single most narrated beat in this run into a clickable one, and would also

**The suprarenal veins** are the cheapest high-value pair after that: two small meshes that would make the
adrenal scene's central surgical fact visible instead of spoken.
**Bile duct and portal vein** remain outstanding from runs 30 and 31 and are still named by five scenes.
**A caecum and colon set** remains outstanding from run 29.
**Peritoneal and serous membranes remain a provider question, not an ingest question** — now named by the
kidney scene as well, which cannot show "retroperitoneal" and has to assert it.
**Local ingest of `FMA7204`, `FMA7205`, `FMA15571`, `FMA15629` and `FMA15630`** would yield roughly ten
measured anchors across this topic's scenes and is the cheapest way to make narrated beats clickable.
**Nerve and vein meshes** remain the highest-value acquisition overall, thirty-three runs old and unchanged.

### Choices made without asking

- Psoas is authored with `mode: "contraction_filter"` on beat two and `"associated_organs"` on beat three,
  matching the `views` the curriculum asks for on this structure rather than inventing beat names.
- The lumbar spine is represented by L1, L3 and L5 plus one disc, not all five vertebrae and five discs. The
  point is that psoas arises in two rows from every level; five identical labels would bury it.
- Coccygeus is carried in the pelvis scene purely as a marker for the sacrospinous ligament, which has no
  mesh — the muscle lies directly on the ligament. It is `role: "context"` and the scene does not claim to
  cover `Pelvic diaphragm (levator ani)`, which is the very next structure at the cursor.
- `COMPARE_STRUCTURES` is authored between the two hip bones in the female-versus-male beat even though it
  compares two identical bones. The beat needs a visual anchor and the op is honest about what it lights;
  the gap says plainly that the dimorphism is narrated.
- Piriformis, obturator internus, coccygeus and the iliac arteries are right-sided only. Their left
  counterparts exist, but the teaching points — the foramina and the brim — are not side-specific and the
  bone has to stay visible.

### Still waiting

**Nerve meshes are now the top gap for the third topic running and are thirty-five runs old.** The lumbar
plexus scene that beat four should be cannot exist: seven named nerves, zero models. Two nerves in the
entire catalog are optic.

**Pelvic ligaments are the new second gap.** Sacrospinous and sacrotuberous alone would make the greater and
lesser sciatic foramina real objects instead of narrated spaces, and those foramina and their contents are
the most examined material in the pelvis. They would serve `Bony pelvis`, `Pelvic diaphragm (levator ani)`
and the gluteal scenes at once.

**A female pelvis set** — named at run 34 and still outstanding — is now blocking two things rather than
one: `Internal reproductive organs`, and the male-versus-female comparison that beat five of the bony pelvis
scene has to speak instead of show.

**Local ingest of `FMA16586`, `FMA16587` and `FMA16202`** would yield roughly a dozen measured anchors — ASIS,
PSIS, ischial spine, ischial tuberosity, pubic tubercle, sacral promontory — and would turn three beats of a
bone scene from recited lists into clickable points. For a scene about a shape, that is close to the whole
value, and it is the cheapest high-value work available in this topic.

**The gonadal veins** are named by five scenes now. Still two small meshes.

51 / 7073).

### State

`node viz-training/tools/sync-state.mjs` re-run → reconciled. **79 structures covered by 74 scene files.**
Gross Anatomy **58 of 81**. 21 structures still HELD, unchanged — this run neither added a candidate nor
released one. Next at the cursor: `gross / Pelvis & Perineum / Internal reproductive organs`.

### Choices made without asking

- **The pelvic diaphragm scene claims only `Pelvic diaphragm (levator ani)`.** It shows the bladder, the
  prostate, the rectum and the external anal sphincter, but as context or as things passing through the
  floor. Likewise the bladder scene shows pubococcygeus but does not claim the diaphragm.
- **Both scenes carry the same viscera at different roles.** Rectum, urethra and external anal sphincter are
  `part` in the floor scene because the floor is taught through what passes through it; the bladder is
  `context` there and `part` in its own scene. The duplication is deliberate — a student who opens the floor
  scene must be able to click the anal canal.
- **Beat names follow the curriculum's `views` for each structure** rather than inventing beat modes:
  `location`, `contraction_filter`, `mechanism` for the diaphragm; `location`, `cross_section`, `mechanism`
  for the bladder.
- **`TRACE_STRUCTURE` targets are concepts, not meshes**, where the subject has no model:
  `concept:faeces`, `concept:intra-abdominal-pressure`, `concept:urine`, `concept:micturition-reflex`,
  `concept:obstruction`. Each is declared in the op and explained in the narration.
- **Beat 4 of the bladder scene uses `SHOW_RELATIONSHIP` from the sacrum to the bladder** to stand for the
  S2–S4 parasympathetic supply. It lights a bone where a nerve should be. This is a proxy and the gap says so
  plainly; it was authored because the nerve supply is the beat and a beat with no visual anchor at all is
  worse.
- **Piriformis is included in the floor scene as `context` and narrated as a trap** — it is a wall muscle,
  not a floor muscle, and students routinely list it as part of the pelvic diaphragm.
- **Both scenes are male-viscera scenes with female anatomy narrated.** Not a choice so much as the only
  option; recorded here so that when a female set arrives, these two scenes are the first to revisit.

### Still waiting

**The female pelvis set is now the top gap in the corpus, ahead of nerves.** At run 34 it blocked one
structure. It now degrades three authored scenes — bony pelvis (the male-versus-female comparison), pelvic
diaphragm (pubovaginalis, the vaginal walls, prolapse, obstetric floor injury) and urinary bladder (two
peritoneal pouches, the short female urethra) — and it wholly blocks `Internal reproductive organs`, which is
the very next structure at the cursor. Uterus, vagina, ovary, uterine tube. Four meshes.

**Nerve meshes, thirty-six runs old.** This run it cost the innervation of the pelvic floor and the entire
autonomic supply of the bladder, both of which are examined every year.

**The perineal body and the perineal membrane** would together make the second diaphragm real and would serve
the perineum scenes that this topic still owes.

**Local ingest of `FMA15900` with measured anchors on the base** would place three points — two ureteric
orifices and the internal urethral orifice — and turn the trigone from a recited triangle into a clickable
cystoscopic view. Cheapest high-value work available in this topic, and it does not need a new model.

**`scenes/__tmp-regression.json` should be moved to `tools/fixtures/`.** Awaiting a human decision.

  mechanism of varicocele is words only.
- **Corpus spongiosum** (although the corpus cavernosum and glans exist), **bulbourethral glands**,
  **verumontanum**, **prostatic utricle**, **ejaculatory ducts**, **prostatic zones**, **tunica vaginalis**,
  **seminiferous tubules**, **rete testis**.
- **Every named branch of the internal iliac artery.** Superior and inferior gluteal, internal pudendal,
  obturator, umbilical, superior and inferior vesical, middle rectal, uterine, vaginal, iliolumbar, lateral
  sacral, median sacral — searched by name, none present. The trunk exists and ends without dividing.
- **All pelvic venous plexuses** — vesical, prostatic, uterine, rectal, presacral, vertebral. The valveless
  plexus is the whole idea of beat four of the vessels scene and there is nothing to show it on.
- **Sacrospinous and sacrotuberous ligaments**, so the two sciatic foramina are described as open notches.
- **Inferior epigastric artery / aberrant obturator artery**, so the corona mortis is a warning with no
  picture.
- **All nerves. Thirty-seven runs.** Obturator, pudendal, sciatic, lumbosacral trunk, sacral plexus, pelvic
  splanchnics, hypogastric nerves — the catalog still holds two nerves and both are optic.
- **All lymph nodes**, so the para-aortic-versus-inguinal drainage of the testis — arguably the single most
  examined fact in the reproductive topic — is traced along arteries as stand-in waypoints.

All of the above are written into `gaps[]` on the scene that needed them, not summarised away here.

### Validator

`node viz-training/tools/validate-scenes.mjs --mark` → **76 of 77 scenes valid.** Both new scenes pass all
eight stages: schema, canonical, provider-id, existence, ops, capability, purity, lifecycle. The only
degraded op on each is `PEEL_LAYER`, authored deliberately. No new scene was marked `blocked`.

The one failure remains `scenes/__tmp-regression.json`, the deliberately broken fixture, producing **10**
errors across existence, canonical, ops and purity. Noting again what run 36 noted: the spec says this
fixture must produce **11** errors across five stages, and it currently produces 10 across four. Either the
fixture has drifted or the spec figure is stale — worth a human eye, because that fixture is the only thing
testing the gate itself. Still not moved out of `scenes/`; still awaiting a decision.

### Index

`node viz-training/tools/build-scene-index.mjs` → **index.json written · 77 scenes (54 ready) · 7616 term
mappings** (up from 75 / 53 / 7320).

### Sync after

`node viz-training/tools/sync-state.mjs` → 81 structures covered by 76 scene files. Gross Anatomy **59 of
81**, 22 held. STATE.json reconciled; not hand-edited. **The gross worklist is now walked to its end** and
the cursor has moved to `embryology / Gametogenesis & Fertilization / Spermatogenesis & oogenesis`.

### Choices made without asking

- **`Internal reproductive organs` is `candidate`, not `ready`, and that is the significant decision of this
  run.** The curriculum entry says "male/female organs in situ". The male half resolves to nine good meshes;
  the female half resolves to none at all. Marking it `ready` would have credited the entry in
  `coveredStructures`, removed it from the worklist, and made the missing female pelvis invisible to every
  future run — which is precisely the failure `covers[]` exists to prevent. `candidate` keeps it on the HELD
  list, stops a future run authoring a duplicate male scene, and puts the choice in front of a human: split
  the curriculum entry into male and female, or source four female meshes. The alternative reading — that
  the scene should not declare `covers` at all — was rejected because that invites exactly the duplication
  `sync-state.mjs` exists to stop.
- **The vessels scene IS `ready` despite having no branch meshes**, because the spec's option (b) genuinely
  applies here: every branch can be taught through an adjacent structure that exists, and the scene does
  that systematically rather than gesturing at it. Piriformis sorts the gluteal and pudendal vessels;
  obturator internus carries the two canals; the hip bone carries the foramina; the viscera carry their own
  supply. A student finishes the scene able to say where each branch goes and which hole it uses. That is
  the teaching the curriculum asks for, and the missing branch geometry is recorded in `gaps[]` rather than
  used as grounds to downgrade.
- **`FMA3784` "descending aorta" used as the abdominal aorta**, as the `Abdominal aorta & IVC` scene already
  does. Consistent across the corpus, and the substitution is named in `gaps[]`.
- **The reproductive scene uses SHOW_RELATIONSHIP from the seminal vesicle to the ureter** as the nearest
  available proxy for the vas crossing the ureter. It is a crossing the student cannot actually see, and the
  gap says so.
- **Beat modes follow the curriculum's `views`**: `location`, `cross_section`, `associated_organs` for the
  reproductive organs; `vasculature` throughout for the vessels.
- **`TRACE_STRUCTURE` targets are declared concepts** where the subject has no mesh: `concept:sperm`,
  `concept:lymph`, `concept:arterial-flow`, `concept:venous-return`, `concept:collateral-flow`. Each is
  explained in the narration of its beat.
- **Female anatomy is narrated in both scenes** — the pouch of Douglas, the uterine artery crossing the
  ureter, the uterine and vaginal branches of the internal iliac — over male viscera, because a Nigerian
  MBBS student is examined on it and silence would be worse. Recorded so these scenes are revisited first
  when a female set arrives.

### Still waiting

**The female pelvis set is the top gap in the corpus and has now stopped being a degradation and become a
blocker.** At run 34 it cost a comparison. It now degrades four authored scenes — bony pelvis, pelvic
diaphragm, urinary bladder, internal iliac vessels — and it is the sole reason `Internal reproductive
organs` could not be released as ready. Four meshes: uterus, uterine tube, ovary, vagina.

**Vas deferens.** A pair of meshes that would turn the male reproductive scene from a set of organs into a
tract. Cheapest high-value model request the corpus has.

**Branches of the internal iliac artery**, or failing that, **anchors on the trunk** marking the anterior and
posterior division origins. The second needs no new model and would carry beats two and five.

**Nerve meshes, thirty-seven runs old.** This run it cost the entire autonomic and somatic supply of the
male genital tract and every nerve travelling with the internal iliac vessels — obturator, pudendal,
sciatic, lumbosacral trunk.

**Gross Anatomy is now walked end to end.** 59 of 81 ready, 22 held, and the next run crosses into
Embryology, whose 46 structures have no scene at all and whose `preferred_modes` lead with `sequence` and
`diagram` rather than `3d_anatomy`. That is a different engine and a different authoring shape; the first
embryology run should expect to be writing SVG-mode scenes with `structures[]` describing labelled regions
and no refs. Worth a human glance before it starts, because it is the first genuine mode switch in the
corpus.

**`scenes/__tmp-regression.json` should be moved to `tools/fixtures/`, and its error count reconciled with
the spec's stated 11.** Awaiting a human decision, second run running.

## Run 38 — 2026-08-28 · `Spermatogenesis & oogenesis`, `Fertilization`

**Driven by:** the cursor. `DEMAND.json` still does not exist in the repo — checked explicitly with `ls
viz-training/` and with `cat DEMAND.json`, both before reading anything else. So there was no evidence to
override curriculum order with, and both structures came from the cursor: `embryology / Gametogenesis &
Fertilization`, positions 1 and 2 of 4. **This is the first run outside Gross Anatomy.**

### Sync first

`node viz-training/tools/sync-state.mjs` was the first command of the run, before the spec, the curriculum or
any scene was read. It reported 81 structures covered by 76 scene files, Gross Anatomy 59 of 81, 22 held,
Embryology 0 of 46, and `embryology / Gametogenesis & Fertilization / Spermatogenesis & oogenesis` next at
the cursor. STATE.json was already in step — no drift. It also repeated the standing report that
`__tmp-regression.json` matches no curriculum structure.

### Structures attempted

**1 · Spermatogenesis & oogenesis** — `preferred_modes: ["sequence", "diagram"]`. First mode an engine can
render is `sequence`, taken as the structure's **first** choice rather than as a fallback; `diagram` recorded
in `deferred_modes`. 20 regions, 18 parts, 8 views, 39 ops, **planned**.

**2 · Fertilization** — `preferred_modes: ["sequence", "diagram"]`. Same disposition. 16 regions, 14 parts,
8 views, 37 ops, **planned**.

### Models resolved

**None, and none were sought.** Both structures route to `sequence`, so per the authoring contract they are
`provider.primary: "svg"` scenes whose `structures[]` describe labelled regions and carry no `refs`. The
validator skips the canonical, provider-id and existence stages for any scene whose mode is not
`3d_anatomy`, and `available-meshes.json` was not consulted for either scene. Nothing was invented, because
nothing was resolved.

This is the shape the run-37 log predicted for the first embryology run, and it held.

### Models NOT found

Not applicable this run — no mesh was required by either scene. The standing gaps from Gross Anatomy are
unchanged and unaddressed: the female internal genital set, the vas deferens, branches of the internal iliac
artery, and nerve meshes now thirty-eight runs old.

What is missing here is a different resource: **SVG artwork**. Both scenes are complete as semantic layers
and neither can render. Each `gaps[]` entry names the exact drawing that would release it:

- *Spermatogenesis & oogenesis* — a seminiferous tubule in cross-section with the four germ-cell generations
  stacked basement-membrane to lumen, the Sertoli cell spanning them with its tight junctions drawn, the
  Leydig cell in the interstitium, and a parallel female column from oogonium to ovum with both polar bodies
  and the follicle stages beside it.
- *Fertilization* — an oocyte with corona radiata and zona pellucida drawn as separate coats, a sperm shown
  before and after the acrosome reaction, cortical granules under the oolemma, the second polar body in the
  perivitelline space, two pronuclei approaching a single spindle, and a small tube inset marking the ampulla.

### Validator result

`node viz-training/tools/validate-scenes.mjs --mark` → **78/79 scenes valid.** Both new scenes passed all
eight stages on the first attempt, with warnings only:

| stage | spermatogenesis-oogenesis | fertilization |
|---|---|---|
| 1 schema | pass | pass |
| 2 canonical | skipped (mode ≠ 3d_anatomy) | skipped |
| 3 provider-id | skipped | skipped |
| 4 existence | skipped | skipped |
| 5 ops | pass — 39 ops, every target resolves | pass — 37 ops, every target resolves |
| 6 capability | warnings only (below) | warnings only |
| 7 purity | pass — no URL, extension, host, library or provider name | pass |
| 8 lifecycle | pass — `planned`, `provenance.author: "task"` | pass |

The single failure is the known `__tmp-regression.json` fixture, **10 errors** — unchanged, and still the
open discrepancy against the spec's stated 11. It was already `blocked` and `--mark` did not alter that.

**Capability warnings are the honest cost of the mode switch and are worth reading rather than ignoring.**
The `svg` adapter declares `native: []` and `degraded: []`, so every one of the 76 ops across the two scenes
warns *"unknown to the svg adapter — it will be ignored, not degraded"*. That is not a defect in these
scenes; it is the SVG engine having no declared capability table yet. The twelve existing `svg` scenes in
Gross Anatomy produce the same warning. **It should be fixed once, in the adapter, not scene by scene**: as
long as `svg` declares nothing, the capability stage cannot tell a well-authored beat from a nonsense one,
and the corpus is accruing SVG scenes at a rate that makes that silence more expensive each run.

### Index result

`node viz-training/tools/build-scene-index.mjs` → **index.json written · 79 scenes (54 ready) · 7912 term
mappings**, up 296 from 7616. Both new scenes are heavily termed on purpose — this is the first content in
the corpus a preclinical note will match on, and the vocabulary a Nigerian MBBS note actually uses is in
there: *2n*, *1n*, *dictyate arrest*, *Graafian follicle*, *69,XXY*, *ICSI*, *ZP3*, *IZUMO1*.

### Sync again

`node viz-training/tools/sync-state.mjs` re-run last. 83 structures covered by 78 scene files, Embryology
still **0 of 46 ready** with both new structures listed among the now-24 HELD, and the cursor advanced to
`embryology / Gametogenesis & Fertilization / Cleavage & morula`. STATE.json was not hand-edited.

### Decisions worth recording

- **`sequence` was taken as a first choice, not a downgrade.** Neither structure lists `3d_anatomy` at any
  position, so nothing was deferred except `diagram`. There was no temptation to reach for a mesh here and
  none was reached for.
- **Ploidy is written into the label**, not left to the narration — "Primary spermatocyte (2n, 4C)". A
  convention for the rest of Embryology, recorded in `gaps[]` so the next run adopts or changes it knowingly.
- **The two germ lines are separate groups** so `ISOLATE_REGION` can run one column at a time. Beat 8 is the
  only beat that shows both, and it is the beat that compares them.
- **`CROSS_SECTION` is authored in beat 2 of the gametogenesis scene** even though an SVG drawing is already
  a section, so the op will be inert. Kept because it is the correct instruction the day the same scene runs
  on a tubule model — the same reasoning that keeps `TRACE_STRUCTURE` and `PEEL_LAYER` in 3d scenes.
- **`TRACE_STRUCTURE` targets are declared concepts**: `concept:male-germ-line`, `concept:female-germ-line`,
  `concept:sperm`, `concept:zygote-formation`. In a sequence scene the moving subject is usually the process
  itself, which by definition has no drawn object. Each is explained in its beat's narration.
- **The boundary between the two scenes is deliberate.** Gametogenesis ends at the metaphase II arrest;
  fertilization opens by releasing it. The second polar body is narrated in both because the student meets it
  from both directions, and the fertilization scene stops at the first cleavage spindle because
  `Cleavage & morula` is the next curriculum entry.
- **Hormonal control is carried inside the Sertoli and Leydig narration**, not given its own group. The
  hypothalamic-pituitary-gonadal axis is physiology; if a later curriculum entry claims it, the content
  should move rather than be duplicated.
- **`SRY` is in `match.terms` of the fertilization scene** although gonadal differentiation belongs to a
  later structure. Nothing else covers the term today, so a highlighted `SRY` finds this scene; the mapping
  should be moved when the genital system topic is authored. Recorded so it is not left to rot.

### Still waiting

**SVG artwork is now the binding constraint on the corpus, and it changed character this run.** In Gross
Anatomy it held back 12 scenes that were routed to `diagram` because no mesh existed — a fallback. In
Embryology it holds back scenes taking their *first* preferred mode. All 46 embryology structures and most of
the 46 histology ones lead with `sequence`, `diagram` or `microscopic`. On this run's evidence, **every
remaining structure in two of the four courses will land as `planned` on artwork**, and the corpus will keep
producing complete, validated, unrenderable scenes until either drawings exist or the SVG engine can generate
from `structures[]` and `views[]` directly. That is the decision a human should take before many more runs
go by, because it is now the difference between the corpus growing and the product growing.

**The `svg` adapter's capability table is empty** and should be filled in `viz3d.js` and mirrored in
`validate-scenes.mjs`. 14 scenes now warn on every single op. Cheap, and it restores a validator stage that
is currently doing nothing for a seventh of the corpus.

**`scenes/__tmp-regression.json` should be moved to `tools/fixtures/`, and its error count reconciled with
the spec's stated 11 — it produces 10.** Third run reported, still awaiting a human decision. It is also the
one scene `sync-state.mjs` reports as matching no curriculum structure, so it costs a false line in every
run's coverage report.

**The female pelvis set** — uterus, uterine tube, ovary, vagina — is unchanged from run 37 and remains the
top mesh gap. Note that it now has a second claimant: the follicle and ovary regions of this run's
gametogenesis scene would gain from an ovary model if that scene were ever promoted to `3d_anatomy`, though
the curriculum does not currently offer it that mode.

### Decisions worth recording

- **`covers[]` was kept to one name each.** The blastocyst scene narrates apposition, adhesion and the first
  syncytiotrophoblast invasion, because that *is* day six, but it does not claim `Implantation` — trophoblast
  invasion proper, the lacunar stage and the bilaminar disc belong to the next topic. Claiming it would have
  hidden three structures behind one scene that cannot teach them.
- **Twinning is deliberately narrated in both scenes.** Splits before day three give dichorionic diamniotic
  twins and splits after give monochorionic ones, so the rule only makes sense from both ends. There is no
  twinning entry in `CURRICULUM.json`, so neither scene claims one; the terms are in both `match.terms` so a
  highlighted "monochorionic" finds the scene that dates it.
- **The no-growth comparison is a constraint on the artwork, not just on the narration.** Beat 2 of the
  cleavage scene compares the zygote with the eight-cell embryo to show they are the same size. If the
  drawing enlarges later stages for legibility, the beat teaches the opposite of what it says. Written into
  `gaps[]` as a requirement so the illustrator is not left to infer it.
- **Cell counts were put in the cleavage labels and left out of the blastocyst labels.** Run 38 set the
  convention that the examinable number goes in the label. Morula counts (12–32) are quoted consistently
  enough to follow it; blastocyst counts are not, so quoting one would have manufactured a fact. The
  divergence is recorded in `gaps[]` rather than left to look like an oversight.
- **`CROSS_SECTION` authored twice more** (cut morula, cut blastocyst) on the same reasoning as run 38: an
  SVG drawing is already a section so the op is inert today, but it is the correct instruction the day these
  scenes render on a model.

### `sync-state.mjs` (second run)

`87 covered by 82 scene file(s)`, STATE.json reconciled, cursor advanced to
`embryology / Weeks 1-2: Implantation & Bilaminar Disc / Amniotic cavity & yolk sac`. Held count is now 28:
the two new scenes join the worklist as `planned` rather than being marked done, which is correct and is why
the embryology bar still reads 0/46.

### Decisions worth recording

- **A gap written by run 39 was discharged by this run.** The blastocyst scene's `gaps[]` said
  cytotrophoblast and syncytiotrophoblast should be authored as regions in the implantation scene rather than
  expanded there. They are, and the disc scene deliberately does not repeat them — it names them only inside
  the three-layer definition of the chorion. This is the mechanism working as intended: a note left for a
  future run was found and acted on, instead of the same content being written twice at different depths.
- **`covers[]` kept to one name each, and two entries in the same topic were explicitly refused.** The disc
  scene describes the amniotic cavity, both yolk sacs and the chorionic cavity, because a bilaminar disc
  cannot be taught without them. It does **not** claim `Amniotic cavity & yolk sac` or
  `Chorion & placenta (early)`. `gaps[]` names precisely what those scenes would still owe — the amnion
  growing to obliterate the chorionic cavity, the vitelline duct and Meckel's diverticulum, the allantois;
  the villous tree, the placental barrier, maternal-fetal exchange. Naming a cavity is not teaching it, and
  claiming it here would have hidden two structures behind a scene that cannot examine them.
- **The implantation scene's clinical beat is the payload, not an appendix.** Ectopic pregnancy, placenta
  praevia, the accreta spectrum and pre-eclampsia are all authored as consequences of one property of the
  trophoblast, in a single `COMPARE_STRUCTURES` between wrong site and wrong depth. None has a curriculum
  entry in Embryology and none is claimed; the terms are in `match.terms` so a highlighted "accreta" or
  "spiral arteries" in a note lands on the mechanism rather than on a disease page.
- **Disc orientation written into `gaps[]` as an artwork constraint.** Same reasoning as run 39's
  no-growth constraint on the cleavage drawing: beat 1 teaches epiblast-up, and an illustrator who flips the
  disc between panels for layout reasons would invert the one fact the rest of the scene depends on. Stated
  as a requirement so it is not left to be inferred.
- **Two numbering systems kept apart.** The receptive window is given in *cycle* days (about 20–24) because
  that is how it is used clinically, while everything else is in *embryonic* days from fertilization. The
  divergence is flagged in `gaps[]` so the artwork does not put both on one timeline.
- **Day numbers narrated as approximations.** Apposition ~d6, adhesion d6–7, invasion d7–9, lacunae d8–9,
  maternal blood in the lacunae d11–12, fully embedded d12, primary villi d13; disc d8, amniotic cavity d8,
  primary yolk sac d9, extraembryonic mesoderm ~d11, chorionic cavity and secondary yolk sac d12–13,
  prechordal plate ~d14. Published figures for the secondary yolk sac vary by a day either way, so no single
  number is stated as fact.
- **The 14-day rule was included deliberately.** It is raised against exactly this stage in Nigerian MBBS
  ethics teaching, and it gives a student a reason to remember when the primitive streak appears. It is
  narrated as a research limit, not as a claim about the law in any particular country.

**SVG artwork is the whole story for Embryology, and the debt is now six scenes deep.** Topic 1 is complete
and topic 2 is half authored: six structures, six scenes, six `planned`, zero renderable. Runs 38, 39 and 40
have each said this and each said it about more scenes than the last. The two ways out are unchanged —
drawings get made, or the SVG engine learns to render from `structures[]` and `views[]` directly. Every
remaining embryology structure and most histology ones will land the same way, so the cost of not choosing
compounds every run.

**The `svg` adapter's capability table is still empty** in `viz3d.js` and in `validate-scenes.mjs`. 18 scenes

### Decisions worth recording

- **Two gaps written by run 40 were both discharged by this run.** Run 40's bilaminar disc scene listed
  precisely what these two entries would still owe: the amnion obliterating the chorionic cavity, the
  vitelline duct, Meckel's diverticulum, the allantois; the villous tree, the placental barrier and
  maternal-fetal exchange. All six are authored here. Second consecutive run in which a note left for a
  future run was found and acted on instead of the content being written twice at different depths.
- **The topic is now complete, and the completion is honest.** All 4 structures in
  `Weeks 1-2: Implantation & Bilaminar Disc` have scenes, no scene claims a `covers` name it cannot teach,
  and all 4 sit `planned` rather than being quietly promoted. The topic is authored, not delivered, and the
  bar reads 0/46 for exactly that reason.
- **Two artwork constraints written into `gaps[]` rather than left to inference.** The folding panel in the
  amnion scene must be drawn transverse, not sagittal — the vitelline duct's hourglass waist does not exist
  in a sagittal cut and the beat has nothing to show without it. The villus maturation strip in the chorion
  scene must be drawn at one magnification across all three panels — enlarging each stage to fit its label
  would show a villus growing rather than gaining a core, which inverts the only fact in the beat. Same
  class of constraint as run 39's no-growth rule and run 40's disc-orientation rule; three runs have now
  each found one, which suggests stating them is worth doing systematically rather than when noticed.
- **Ordering taught as the fact, twice.** Beat 3 of the chorion scene teaches that the lacunae fill with
  maternal blood *before* any villus exists, because a student who learns the order backwards imagines two
  vessel beds being joined and cannot then explain why the circulations never mix. Beat 2 of the amnion
  scene does the same for the two cavities: one is a space that opened, the other a space that was lined.
  Both are authored as relationships between regions rather than as narration, because the error each
  corrects is a comparison error, not a missing fact.
- **Clinical payload derived, not appended.** Pre-eclampsia and growth restriction are authored as the
  consequence of shallow spiral artery invasion in beat 7; Potter sequence is authored as the consequence of
  what amniotic fluid was for, with the lungs named as the cause of death rather than the kidneys. Neither
  has a curriculum entry in Embryology and neither is claimed in `covers`; the terms sit in `match.terms` so
  a highlighted "pre-eclampsia" or "oligohydramnios" in a note lands on the mechanism.
- **Placental malaria named separately from TORCH.** It is a leading cause of low birth weight in this
  region and Nigerian MBBS teaching covers it alongside that group, but it is not part of the acronym, so
  the narration names it on its own line and `gaps[]` records why.
- **Day numbers narrated as approximations, and two ranges flagged as genuinely disputed.** Amniotic cavity
  ~d8, primary yolk sac d9, secondary yolk sac d12–13, allantois ~d16, vitelline duct closing weeks 6–10;
  lacunae d9, maternal blood in them d11–12, primary villus d13, secondary d16, tertiary ~d21. Two figures
  are flagged in `gaps[]` as varying between texts rather than being stated as fact: chorionic cavity
  obliteration (about week 8 to week 12) and the disappearance of the villous cytotrophoblast (fourth month
  onwards). Spiral artery invasion is taught as one process with `gaps[]` noting the two-wave account and
  that the second wave's timing is still debated — the scene teaches the consequence of shallow invasion
  rather than committing to a wave count.
- **Term placental gross anatomy deliberately not taught.** The curriculum entry says "early". Weight,
  cotyledon count and the maternal/fetal surfaces are in `match.terms` so a note can find the scene, but no
  beat teaches them; `gaps[]` records that they belong to a later fetal membranes topic if one is added.

### What this run decided

- **The topic was split along the seam students actually confuse, not along the obvious one.** The streak
  scene teaches *how cells get out of the epiblast*; the disc scene teaches *what the three sheets become*.
  The alternative split — "week 3 anatomy" and "week 3 clinical" — would have put ingression and germ layer
  origin in different scenes, and the single most-missed fact in this topic sits exactly on that join.
- **That fact is authored twice, deliberately, and in different forms.** Definitive endoderm is epiblast that
  displaced the hypoblast; the hypoblast contributes nothing to the fetus. In the streak scene it is a beat
  about two waves of migration (beat 4, a `TRACE_STRUCTURE` along the two routes). In the disc scene it is a
  beat about origin (beat 2, three `SHOW_RELATIONSHIP` arrows from one region). Same fact, two different
  errors corrected — a movement error and an origin error. This is the one duplication in the run and it is
  intentional; nothing else is repeated between the two scenes.
- **Four curriculum entries were explicitly refused.** `Notochord` and `Neurulation (neural plate/tube)` are
  in this same topic and both scenes name their subjects in passing — the notochordal process because the
  primitive pit has no other purpose, the neural plate because every text lists it under ectoderm — but
  neither is claimed in `covers[]` and `gaps[]` says why on both scenes. `Body cavity (coelom)` from the
  Folding topic is likewise refused although the intraembryonic coelom is named in beat 6 of the disc scene.
  Naming a structure inside another structure's explanation is not teaching it.
- **The notochord is a `context` region in the disc scene, not a `part`.** Paraxial mesoderm is *defined* by
  its position beside the notochord, so beat 5's transverse cut is unreadable without the midline drawn. A
  `context` role puts it in the picture without putting it in the student's list of things this scene claims
  to teach — which is exactly the distinction `role` exists for, and the honest way to draw something you
  are not covering.
- **Two artwork constraints written into `gaps[]` rather than left to inference.** The streak scene's dorsal
  view must keep the cranial end at the top in every panel, because beats 1, 6 and 8 all assume the reader
  knows which end is caudal without being told again. The disc scene's transverse panel must show *both*
  sides of the midline — drawing one half to save space would make three bilateral columns read as a stack,
  and the paired somites in beat 6 would have nothing to stand on. Fourth run running to find exactly one
  such constraint per scene; the pattern is now consistent enough that it is worth adding a standing
  artwork-constraint check to the authoring prompt rather than relying on each run noticing.
- **Clinical payload derived, not appended.** Kartagener syndrome is authored as the consequence of nodal
  cilia failing (beat 7), so situs inversus and bronchiectasis arrive in the same patient for a stated
  reason. Sacrococcygeal teratoma and caudal dysgenesis are both authored as the streak's regression going
  wrong in opposite directions (beat 8) — too much left behind, too little made. Teratoma composition
  appears again in the disc scene, but as the *definition* question rather than the *site* question: hair is
  ectoderm, cartilage is mesoderm, thyroid epithelium is endoderm. Neither has a curriculum entry in
  Embryology and neither is claimed; the terms sit in `match.terms`.
- **Maternal diabetes named as the association for caudal dysgenesis, with the timing spelled out.** Poor
  periconceptional glycaemic control, not control after a positive test — the narration states the window
  because that is what makes the fact clinically useful rather than a list item.
- **Molecular detail kept deliberately shallow.** FGF8, Brachyury and E-cadherin are in `match.terms`; only
  FGF8 and E-cadherin appear in narration, and only inside the EMT sentence. `gaps[]` records that Nigerian
  MBBS papers name these but do not examine the pathways, and that the narration should not grow if a
  molecular topic is added later.
- **Derivative lists are incomplete on purpose and `gaps[]` says so.** They cover what is asked to be
  reproduced. A list a student cannot finish is worse than a shorter one they can, and padding it would
  make the beat unspeakable at the length the narration is written for.
- **Neural crest is narrated inside ectoderm rather than given its own region.** Some texts teach it as a
  fourth germ layer. The scene follows the majority convention, and `gaps[]` records both the choice and
  what should change if a neural crest curriculum entry is ever added.
- **Day numbers narrated as approximations.** Streak appears ~d15, somites from ~d20, streak regression
  largely complete by ~d26, trilaminar disc from ~d16. Texts differ by a day or two at each and no figure is
  stated as fact.

### Notes from this run

- **The notochord scene is built around one correction: it is hollow before it is solid.** Four named
  stages — process, canal, plate, definitive rod — authored as a single `TRACE_STRUCTURE` through
  `concept:prenotochordal-cells` rather than four separate facts. A student who pictures a rod on day 16 has
  nowhere to put the notochordal plate, cannot say why it briefly forms part of the yolk-sac roof, and
  cannot explain the neurenteric canal at all. Every later beat in that scene depends on getting this right.
- **The neurenteric canal is authored as normal, then reused as the mechanism of a malformation.** It is
  drawn as a transient communication that closes, and beat 8 derives split notochord syndrome and the
  neurenteric cyst from it failing to. Deriving the lesion from the normal event is the pattern this corpus
  has settled on; a malformation appended as a list item teaches nothing about why it exists.
- **`PEEL_LAYER` authored twice, degrading in both places, deliberately.** Notochord beat 7 strips the
  vertebral bodies off the surviving nucleus pulposus; neurulation beat 8 lifts skin off an occult lesion.
  In the second case the degraded rendering still carries the point, because "the skin is what hides this
  one" is precisely what separates spina bifida occulta from an open defect and from a raised AFP.
- **The preposition is the teaching point in notochord beat 6.** The vertebral column forms *around* the
  notochord, not *from* it. The commonest wrong answer in this topic is a one-word substitution, so the
  narration says it twice and `COMPARE_STRUCTURES` puts the two inductions — upward to ectoderm, sideways to
  sclerotome — beside each other rather than in sequence.
- **Three dates carry the whole clinical half of the neurulation scene.** Closure begins at the fifth somite
  about day 22; cranial neuropore about day 25; caudal about day 27. Anencephaly is then derived from the
  first date missed and the spina bifida spectrum from the second, and beat 8's folate argument is nothing
  but the calendar: the tube shuts before most women know they are pregnant.
- **One artwork constraint per scene, again, written into `gaps[]` rather than left to inference.** The
  notochord's median section must keep the cranial end to the left and the amnion above in every panel.
  The neurulation spina bifida panels must be drawn at one scale with the same vertebral outline, because
  the teaching point is how much herniates and differing magnifications destroy the comparison. Fifth run
  running to find exactly one such constraint per scene. Run 42 suggested adding a standing artwork-constraint
  check to the authoring prompt; that suggestion is repeated here with a run of five behind it.
- **Cross-claiming refused in both directions, and the two scenes were written to lean on each other.**
  The notochord scene names neural induction (beat 6) but does not claim `Neurulation`; the neurulation
  scene names the notochord as the inducer (beat 2) but does not claim `Notochord`. Each `gaps[]` says so
  and says what belongs where. Neither claims `Primitive streak` or `Trilaminar disc`, though both name
  them in passing.
- **`Nervous System Development` is a later Embryology topic and was explicitly not encroached on.** The
  neurulation scene stops at tube closure and crest fates. Alar and basal plates, sulcus limitans and brain
  vesicle derivatives are in `match.terms` only, so a highlighted term will reach the right scene once that
  topic is authored — and will fall back to this one until then.
- **Neural crest gets its own region here, unlike in the trilaminar disc scene where it was narrated inside
  ectoderm.** The difference is warranted: in the disc scene the question is which germ layer it comes from,
  and in this scene the question is what happens at the fusing fold tips, which is a distinct event with a
  distinct cell behaviour. The derivative list is authored in full because it is the answer to why
  Hirschsprung, phaeochromocytoma and a facial cleft can share an explanation.
- **Folate doses given as the widely taught 400 µg / 5 mg periconceptional figures, with a caveat in
  `gaps[]`** that national guidance differs and changes and that the scene teaches the timing principle, not
  a prescription. Anencephaly is stated plainly as fatal, because that is the examined fact; management of
  an affected pregnancy is a counselling matter and is deliberately absent.
- **Day numbers narrated as approximations throughout**, except the 25/27 neuropore pair, which is kept
  exact because the two malformations hang on the distinction between them.

`node viz-training/tools/sync-state.mjs` run again afterwards: 95 covered by 90 scene files, `next to

not hand-edited.

### Authoring decisions worth keeping

- **The two folds are one event and were deliberately authored as two scenes in two planes.** Cranio-caudal
  is drawn only in the median plane, lateral only in transverse, and each scene says in narration that the
  other is happening simultaneously. Trying to show both in one diagram is the commonest way students end up
  unable to draw either, and the split follows the curriculum, which lists them as separate structures.
- **The reversal is the scene, not a detail in it.** Beat 2 of the cranio-caudal scene authors the cranial
  strip twice — `head_order_before` and `head_order_after` — and puts them in `COMPARE_STRUCTURES` rather
  than narrating the change. Septum transversum, pericardial cavity, cardiogenic area, oropharyngeal
  membrane becomes oropharyngeal membrane, heart, septum transversum. That flip is the examined point and it
  is the only thing in the topic that must be memorised rather than derived.
- **One artwork constraint per scene, written into `gaps[]` rather than left to inference.** Sixth run
  running. Cranio-caudal: the before/after panels must number the four items identically at one scale.
  Lateral: one colour key for ectoderm, somatic mesoderm, splanchnic mesoderm and endoderm across all three
  transverse panels, because the teaching point is that the same two sheets become body wall and gut wall.
  Runs 42 and 43 each suggested adding a standing artwork-constraint check to the authoring prompt; that
  suggestion is repeated here with a run of six behind it.
- **Folding was authored as a consequence of growth, not an activity.** Both scenes open by establishing the
  drivers — a neural tube outgrowing a yolk sac that does not grow, and an expanding amnion — before any
  fold is drawn. Students who learn folding as a thing the embryo does cannot predict any of the defects;
  students who learn it as what a differentially growing sheet must do can derive most of them.
- **Two facts from elsewhere in the syllabus were paid off here rather than merely mentioned.** The
  C3–C4–C5 innervation of the diaphragm is derived from the septum transversum's journey, and the two-layer
  structure of every serous cavity is derived from the somatopleure/splanchnopleure split. Both are things
  students are usually told to memorise in another course; both are consequences of a fold.
- **Beat 5 of the lateral scene is a differential, not a description.** Omphalocele and gastroschisis are
  compared side by side with three checks in fixed order — membrane, midline, cord insertion — and the
  chromosomal and cardiac association is attached to omphalocele only. This is the pairing most likely to be
  asked and the most likely to be answered backwards.
- **Cross-claiming refused in four directions.** Neither scene claims `Primitive gut tube` or `Body cavity
  (coelom)`, both of which are entries in this same topic and still unauthored; neither claims the other.
  Foregut, midgut and hindgut are named in the cranio-caudal scene because the folds create them, and the
  coelom is named in the lateral scene because folding closes it, but each `gaps[]` states plainly what is
  not claimed and where it belongs. Marking either covered here would have hidden two structures that are
  next on the worklist.
- **Midgut rotation and diaphragm development were named only as far as this topic explains them.**
  Physiological herniation and its return by about week 10 appear because omphalocele is unintelligible
  without them; rotation, malrotation and volvulus are left to GIT Development. The pleuroperitoneal
  membranes are left to a diaphragm scene. Both sets of terms are in `match.terms` so a highlighted term
  reaches these scenes until the proper ones exist.
- **First Embryology structure with `3d_anatomy` in its preferred modes.** `Lateral folding` lists
  `["sequence","diagram","3d_anatomy"]`. Per the prompt the first renderable mode was taken and **both**
  others recorded in `deferred_modes`, with `gaps[]` arguing that the 3d option is real and should be
  revisited the day staged embryo geometry exists. The preferred modes were not downgraded.
- **Week numbers narrated as approximations** — folding through week 4, herniation about week 6 returning
  about week 10 — because nothing here turns on an exact day, unlike the neuropore pair in run 43. The
  Meckel rule of twos is flagged in `gaps[]` as a mnemonic rather than an epidemiological claim.
- **Caudal regression is given with its maternal diabetes association** because that is the examined link.
  As with anencephaly in run 43, the narration names the malformation and its association and does not
  discuss management of an affected pregnancy, which is counselling rather than embryology.

### Still waiting

**SVG artwork is now fourteen scenes deep and remains the entire reason Embryology reads 0/46.** Topics 1, 2
and 3 are complete and topic 4 is half authored: fourteen structures, fourteen scenes, fourteen `planned`,

### Authoring decisions worth recording

- **`covers[]` kept to one entry each, and the overlaps written down.** These four folding scenes describe
  the same few days from different angles, so the risk is not under-claiming but double-claiming. The gut
  tube scene explicitly does NOT claim `Lateral folding`, `Cranio-caudal folding` or `Body cavity (coelom)`;
  the coelom scene does NOT claim the two folding entries or `Primitive gut tube`. Each `gaps[]` names what
  it declines and why. The two folding scenes authored in run 44 had already declined both of this run's
  structures in their own `gaps[]`, which is what made this run's scope unambiguous.
- **The gut tube is taught arterially.** Beat 2 fixes the three arteries and their vertebral levels before a
  single derivative is named, because the divisions, the referred-pain levels and the malformation map all
  hang off them. `gaps[]` requires one colour key for the three divisions across every panel for exactly
  that reason.
- **The coelom is taught as four partitions with four hernias.** Beat 6 maps Bochdalek, Morgagni,
  eventration and the congenital hiatus hernia back onto the partition that failed. `gaps[]` requires the
  partition sequence to *draw* the right pericardioperitoneal canal closing before the left — narrating it
  without drawing it wastes the panel, and it is the entire reason the common CDH is left-sided.
- **Five `concept:` traces authored across the two scenes** — `concept:gut-tube`,
  `concept:midgut-rotation`, `concept:coelom`, `concept:diaphragm-formation`, `concept:phrenic-nerve`. In
  each the subject is a lumen, a movement, a space or a nerve being dragged, none of which is a drawn
  region.
- **`PEEL_LAYER` authored twice** — on `vessel` in the gut tube scene, to take the arteries off the final
  malformation map after they have done their work in beat 2, and on `muscle` in the coelom scene, to lift
  the diaphragm off the hernia map. Both degrade on the current renderer and both are authored anyway.
- **Week and day numbers narrated as approximations throughout**, as in runs 43 and 44 — herniation about
  week 6 returning about week 10, recanalization by about week 8, pleuroperitoneal closure about week 7,
  diaphragm at L1 by about week 8. The left-sided predominance of Bochdalek hernia is given as "the great
  majority" rather than a percentage, because quoted figures range from about 80 to 90 across texts and
  nothing taught here turns on the number.
- **Malformations carry mechanism and classic presentation only.** Operative management is surgery, not
  embryology, and is deliberately absent from both scenes.

### Decisions worth recording

- **Arch 5 is authored as a region with no derivatives rather than omitted.** It carries no cartilage, no
  muscle, no nerve and no artery, and says so. A student who is never shown the gap mis-numbers arch 6 as
  arch 5, and that is a manufactured error the corpus can simply decline to manufacture.
- **The two recurrent laryngeal nerves are taught as one rule, not two facts** — each hooks under the lowest
  arch artery still standing on its own side. `gaps[]` requires the artery panel to draw both sides on one
  figure, because a single-sided drawing hides the asymmetry that is the whole point of the beat.
- **The pouches scene opens by separating endoderm from ectoderm before naming a single derivative**, and
  `gaps[]` requires the two to carry different fills in every panel. Attributing a cleft derivative to a
  pouch is the commonest error in this topic and a drawing that colours both alike teaches it actively.
- **The parathyroid crossover is authored as a before-and-after of the same neck**, not as a static
  labelled picture. It is a change of relative position over time; drawn once it becomes a fact to
  memorise, drawn twice it becomes a consequence, and the ectopic-parathyroid surgery follows from the
  second version only.
- **The C cells are taught as neural crest in origin, and the scene says so explicitly rather than leaving
  it implied.** Texts are not uniform on this; `gaps[]` records the disagreement. The neural crest reading
  is chosen because it is what makes medullary thyroid carcinoma's behaviour — no iodine uptake, calcitonin
  rather than thyroglobulin, MEN 2 — follow rather than be memorised.
- **The rudimentary fifth pouch is treated as part of the fourth**, with the narration noting that texts
  which count it separately credit it with the same ultimopharyngeal body. The derivative is identical
  either way and the scene declines to pick a side it does not need to pick.
- **Four `concept:` traces authored across the two scenes** — `concept:arch-series`,
  `concept:arch-artery-remodelling`, `concept:pouch-series`, `concept:thymic-descent`. In each the subject
  is a progression through a series, a pattern of regression, or a migration over time, none of which is a
  drawn region.
- **`PEEL_LAYER` authored twice** — on `vessel` in the arches scene to take the arch arteries off the
  syndrome map after beat 4 has used them, and on `organ` in the pouches scene because the final panel is
  read twice, once for what forms and once for what is missing. Both degrade on the current renderer and
  both are authored anyway.
- **Neither scene claims the other's territory, and both explicitly decline the next two structures at the
  cursor.** The arches scene names the pouches only as the arch's inner lining; the pouches scene names the
  arches only as the bars it lies between. Both decline "Pharyngeal clefts & membranes" and "Thyroid gland
  development" in `gaps[]`, naming exactly what they left there — cervical sinus remnants, the branchial
  fistula, the external acoustic meatus, the thyroglossal duct, descent from the foramen cecum.
- **Week numbers narrated as approximations throughout**, as in runs 43 to 45 — arches through week 4,
  cervical sinus obliterating about week 7, pouches through weeks 4 and 5, thymic descent complete about
  week 8.
- **Syndromes carry mechanism and classic presentation only.** Management is paediatrics and surgery, not
  embryology, and is deliberately absent from both scenes. The two exceptions in the DiGeorge narration —
  no live vaccines, irradiate blood products — are kept because they follow directly from the absent thymus
  and are the two things that change what happens to the baby on the ward.

## Run 47 — 2026-08-28

**Not demand-driven.** `viz-training/DEMAND.json` does not exist, so the run fell back to the cursor as the
task specifies. `sync-state.mjs` ran first, before anything was read or decided, and reported 99 structures
covered by 94 scene files with the cursor at `embryology / Pharyngeal Apparatus / Pharyngeal clefts &
membranes`; STATE.json was already in step.

**Attempted (2):**

1. `Pharyngeal clefts & membranes` — Embryology / Pharyngeal Apparatus
2. `Thyroid gland development` — Embryology / Pharyngeal Apparatus

Both completed. Both `status: "planned"`. Topic 5 of Embryology is now fully authored.

### Mode

Both structures carry `preferred_modes: ["sequence", "diagram", "3d_anatomy"]`. The first mode an engine can
render today is `sequence`, so both are `sequence` scenes on the `svg` provider with
`deferred_modes: ["diagram", "3d_anatomy"]`.

**The `3d_anatomy` option was deferred, not refused, and the distinction is deliberate.** Both structures have
genuinely spatial adult subject matter — the external acoustic meatus and the tympanic membrane, the tract of
a second-cleft fistula running between the internal and external carotid arteries, the thyroid gland on the
second to fourth tracheal rings, the route of descent past the hyoid. Any of that would be better taught on a
model. The catalog was deliberately **not** consulted for meshes, because mode selection takes the first
renderable preferred mode and does not shop below it; a missing model is not a reason to edit a structure's
preferred modes. Both scenes say this in `gaps[]` so the 3d route stays visible to whoever reads them next.

### Models resolved / not found

**None, and none attempted — correctly.** Neither scene is `3d_anatomy`, so neither carries `refs{}`, and
`available-meshes.json` was not consulted for either. Validator stages 3 (provider-id) and 4 (existence) are
skipped by design for non-3d modes.

### Validator

`node viz-training/tools/validate-scenes.mjs --mark` → **96/97 valid.**

| scene | result |
|---|---|
| `embryology__pharyngeal-apparatus__pharyngeal-clefts-membranes` | ✓ 14 structures (13 parts) · 5 views · 27 ops · planned · 27 capability warnings |
| `embryology__pharyngeal-apparatus__thyroid-gland-development` | ✓ 14 structures (13 parts) · 5 views · 27 ops · planned · 27 capability warnings |

Zero errors across all eight stages for both scenes. The 54 warnings are all stage 6 (capability) and all say
the same thing — the `svg` adapter declares no ops at all, so every op in every SVG scene warns. See "Still
waiting" below.

The only failing file remains `scenes/__tmp-regression.json` (10 errors), which is the deliberate fixture and
is the sole reason the validator exits non-zero.

### Index

`node viz-training/tools/build-scene-index.mjs` → **`index.json` written · 97 scenes (54 ready) · 10543 term
mappings**, up from 10233.

### State

`node viz-training/tools/sync-state.mjs` ran again after writing and **reconciled STATE.json with the scenes
directory**. Cursor advanced to `embryology / Cardiovascular Development / Heart tube formation`. Nothing was
hand-edited. Both new structures are recorded as authored-but-held, which is correct: `planned` is not
`ready`, so they stay visible as unfinished work rather than disappearing off the worklist.

### Authoring notes

- **`covers[]` is one entry per scene, and each scene explicitly declines the other three Pharyngeal
  Apparatus structures in `gaps[]`.** The clefts scene names the first pouch (the tympanic membrane needs
  both sides of the apparatus to exist) and the second pouch (a complete fistula opens into the tonsillar
  fossa), and declines the pouch derivative list, the wings, the thymic descent and DiGeorge. The thyroid
  scene names the fourth pouch and the ultimopharyngeal body (the gland cannot be described honestly without
  saying half its cell types came from elsewhere) and declines everything else. Neither claims
  `Pharyngeal arches`. The topic is now four scenes covering four structures with no overlap and no double
  claim.
- **One deliberate non-duplication.** Medullary thyroid carcinoma and MEN 2 follow from the neural-crest
  origin of the C cells and are taught in the *pouches* scene. The thyroid scene names the two-lineage fact
  and stops, with a `gaps[]` line saying why. The terms stay in the thyroid scene's `match.terms`, so a
  highlighted term still reaches a scene — it just reaches the one that owns the teaching.
- **`TRACE_STRUCTURE` authored five times, always on a `concept:` target.** `concept:operculum-overgrowth`,
  `concept:cleft-series`, `concept:thyroid-descent`, `concept:ectopic-sites` — in each the subject is a
  movement over time or a progression through a series, not a drawn region. No bare word was used as a
  target.
- **`PEEL_LAYER` authored twice**, on `organ` in both scenes, so each clinical panel can be read twice —
  once for what forms, once for what is left behind or missing. Both degrade on the current renderer and both
  are authored anyway.
- **One flagged uncertainty worth a reader's eye.** The thyroid scene's beat 4 uses `CROSS_SECTION` on the
  `z` axis for a sagittal cut through the midline neck. The op and its intent are right; the axis letter is
  the one thing a renderer could disagree with, and `gaps[]` says to check it against the artwork's own axis
  convention rather than assume it.
- **Two places where texts genuinely differ are narrated as differences, not resolved silently.** Whether the
  fourth cleft is well formed at all (the derivative claim is unaffected either way, since no lower cleft
  contributes to the adult), and whether the thyroglossal duct passes in front of, behind, or through the
  developing hyoid (the narration gives anterior descent with a common loop behind the body, because that is
  the description that makes the Sistrunk procedure make sense, and says "commonly").
- **Week numbers narrated as approximations throughout**, as in runs 43 to 46 — cervical sinus obliterated
  about week 7, meatal plug recanalising late in fetal life, thyroid primordium about day 24, descent
  complete about week 7, follicles and colloid from about week 11.
- **Clinical content carries mechanism and classic presentation; management appears only where it is the
  embryology.** Two exceptions, both kept because they *are* the anatomical argument: the Sistrunk procedure
  takes the middle third of the hyoid because the bone ossified around the duct, and an ectopic thyroid is
  scanned before excision because it is frequently the patient's only functioning gland.

### Still waiting

through 5 are complete: twenty structures, twenty scenes, twenty `planned`, zero renderable. Runs 38 through
